# Paper-to-formalization references

## RH mixed-channel connections: shifted convolution, Chowla/Elliott, Carleson, and formalized PFR (2026-10-29)

Local attribution: grwtsk contributors. Source commit `25669a6870f1ece21227619a61c91e0799246851`,
module `GRWTSK/NumberTheory/ElementaryNumberTheory/ArithmeticFunctionsRelatedNumbersInversionFormulas/PickupRoughFarMixedChannel.lean`,
blob `f0472a476f5d32162a1fd0d8365325d937ec850f`. No external theorem is imported;
all mappings below are ANALOGY_ONLY and no historical influence is inferred.

- Florea--Lalín--Malik--Sahay, _The shifted convolution problem in function fields_
  (2025), arXiv:2502.16067. Their shifted and mixed correlation results motivate
  treating `mixedGram` as its own correlation object rather than as two unrelated
  Gram terms. No Voronoi/Estermann or function-field theorem is imported.
- Tao, _The logarithmically averaged Chowla and Elliott conjectures for two-point
  correlations_ (2015), arXiv:1509.05422. The analogy is dependence-aware
  multiplicative/prime channel averaging. No entropy decrement, logarithmic
  averaging or Elliott result is imported.
- Becker et al., _Formalizing Carleson's Theorem in Lean_ (2026),
  arXiv:2609.31334. The analogy is explicit localization/channel-mismatch control;
  no Carleson operator or tile estimate is imported.
- Polynomial Freiman--Ruzsa Lean formalization, https://teorth.github.io/pfr/ ,
  based on Gowers--Green--Manners--Tao, arXiv:2311.05762, and later developments.
  The analogy is methodological: any information-theoretic representation needs
  a proved return to the exact `transportedDivisorMask` consumer.

Exact local content: `mixedGram` is the difference
`gram P T m (p*n) - gram P T (p*m) (p*n)`;
`mixedGram_eq_intervalIntegral` identifies it with the mixed block integral using
`block_cons`; `moved_indicator_mismatch_eq_signedChange` returns the selector
mismatch to the existing left-dilation cutoff calculus;
`reducedDivisorMaskAtom_eq_mixedChannel` and
`transportedDivisorMask_eq_mixedChannel` return the pointwise and complete
divisor-mask consumers to mixed-Gram, moved-selector and atomic `1/omega` sectors.

Evidence roles: imported mathematics is existing GRWTSK/Mathlib only; kernel-verified
new bridges none claimed; source proof candidates are the four declarations above;
analogies are exactly the four mappings above. Compilation, fresh kernel replay,
transitive axiom audit and expected-interface checking are NOT_RUN. Missing bridges:
a quantitative mixed-Gram estimate, moved-selector control at `p*n`, atomic
`1/omega` control, and recombination with the geometric and transported-main sectors.
Track verification in https://github.com/grwtsk/math/issues/1490 .

## RH divisor-mask incidence connections: Chowla, Duffin--Schaeffer, and formalized PFR (2026-10-29)

Local attribution: grwtsk contributors. This entry records literature connections to
math source commit `cf25bba53d45989cbba2c02cbbd6a499726a4a06`, module
`GRWTSK/NumberTheory/ElementaryNumberTheory/ArithmeticFunctionsRelatedNumbersInversionFormulas/PickupRoughFarDivisorMaskIncidence.lean`,
blob `ee6b5b0f3e2c95dcd19370715fb14b0418d960b1`.
No theorem from these sources is imported into the local proof. Their role is
ANALOGY / RESEARCH GUIDANCE only; no historical influence is inferred.

- **Terence Tao, _The logarithmically averaged Chowla and Elliott conjectures for
  two-point correlations_ (2015), arXiv:1509.05422.**
  Stable source: https://arxiv.org/abs/1509.05422 .
  The paper proves the logarithmically averaged two-point Chowla/Elliott result
  using small-prime multiplicativity, short-interval input, concentration and an
  entropy decrement argument. The local connection is the exact single-prime
  incidence calculus: multiplying an index by a fresh p changes the selected/kept
  divisor data only at p, with the resulting pairWeight numerator/denominator
  increments explicit. No entropy, mutual-information or logarithmic-average
  estimate is formalized locally. A quantitative bridge from prime-family
  dependence to `transportedDivisorMask` remains unresolved.

- **Scott D. Hughes, _Effective logarithmic two-point Chowla bounds in every
  window_ (2026), arXiv:2609.28526.**
  Stable source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.28526 .
  The paper quantifies the entropy-decrement proof uniformly in affine forms and
  averaging windows. The local source does not import its bounds. The relevance is
  methodological only: a later prime-family estimate could be judged against an
  effective dependence/scale-selection theorem once the exact local incidence
  variables are defined. No such transfer is proved.

- **Dimitris Koukoulopoulos and James Maynard, _On the Duffin-Schaeffer
  conjecture_ (Annals of Mathematics 192 (2020), 251--307),
  DOI 10.4007/annals.2020.192.1.5.**
  Stable source: https://doi.org/10.4007/annals.2020.192.1.5 .
  The paper proves Duffin--Schaeffer using arithmetic overlap control, compression,
  density increment and graph-theoretic structure. The local connection is that
  exceptional overlap is now represented exactly by one-prime changes of
  `keptDivisors` and `omega`, rather than by a free error term. No local theorem
  identifies these inventories with the Duffin--Schaeffer overlap graph or imports
  any compression inequality.

- **Polynomial Freiman--Ruzsa Lean formalization, based on Gowers--Green--Manners--Tao,
  _On a conjecture of Marton_ (2023), arXiv:2311.05762, with subsequent project
  developments.**
  Stable formalization source: https://teorth.github.io/pfr/ .
  The project formalizes the PFR proof and the required Shannon-entropy machinery.
  The local connection is methodological: an information-theoretic representation
  would only be useful after a proved return from a dependence/entropy quantity to
  the explicit divisor-mask contribution. No entropy object or PFR theorem is
  imported into the RH source.

- **Exact local theorem content.** For a fresh prime p and, where stated, Z<p:
  `fresh_dvd_prime_mul_iff` proves all fresh q != p keep their divisibility
  status under m->p*m; `keptDivisors_left_eq_erase` removes only p;
  `selectedDivisors_right_eq_insert` inserts only p under n->p*n;
  `omega_right_eq` records the exact denominator increment;
  `keptDivisors_right_eq` records the exact numerator insertion condition;
  `keptDivisors_both_eq_erase` resolves simultaneous transport;
  `pairWeight_left_sub_eq`, `pairWeight_right_eq`, and
  `pairWeight_both_eq` give the corresponding exact real-valued weights.
- **Evidence roles.** Imported mathematics: existing GRWTSK/Mathlib source only.
  Kernel-verified bridges: none claimed. Source proof candidates: the declarations
  above. Conditional interfaces: the explicit fresh-prime and Z<p hypotheses.
  Analogies: exactly the literature mappings above. Unresolved premises:
  compilation, fresh replay, transitive axiom audit, expected-interface checking,
  substitution into the complete divisor-mask commutator, a quantitative
  prime-family/overlap estimate, and return to the complete far-source consumer.
- **Verification.** Requested commands are
  `lake build GRWTSK.NumberTheory.ElementaryNumberTheory.ArithmeticFunctionsRelatedNumbersInversionFormulas.PickupRoughFarDivisorMaskIncidence`
  and
  `lake env lean --trust=0 -DwarningAsError=true GRWTSK/NumberTheory/ElementaryNumberTheory/ArithmeticFunctionsRelatedNumbersInversionFormulas/PickupRoughFarDivisorMaskIncidence.lean`.
  Compilation NOT_RUN; fresh kernel replay NOT_RUN; transitive axiom audit NOT_RUN;
  expected-interface check NOT_RUN. Track source-bound verification in
  https://github.com/grwtsk/math/issues/1487 .

## RH far-mask commutator connections: Chowla, Duffin--Schaeffer, and Carleson (2026-10-29)

Local attribution: grwtsk contributors. This entry records three literature connections
to the source proof candidate at math `1198aa95fee7aa38718faf8ac8e633a75ef8ffb0`,
module
`GRWTSK/NumberTheory/ElementaryNumberTheory/ArithmeticFunctionsRelatedNumbersInversionFormulas/PickupRoughFarMaskCommutator.lean`,
blob `80651cb82e9a9256a61a2721aae07cfc2abc10b6`.
The local mathematics does not import any theorem from these works. Their role is
ANALOGY / RESEARCH GUIDANCE only; no historical influence is inferred.

- **Terence Tao, _The logarithmically averaged Chowla and Elliott conjectures for
  two-point correlations_ (2015), arXiv:1509.05422.**
  Stable source: https://arxiv.org/abs/1509.05422 .
  The paper proves logarithmically averaged two-point correlation results using,
  among other inputs, multiplicativity at small primes and an entropy decrement
  argument. The local formal connection is only the exact finite fresh-prime-family
  surface
  `GRWTSK.NumberTheory.Moebius.PickupRoughFarMaskCommutator.farNegative_primeFamily_total`.
  This theorem preserves the complete signed source while allowing the transported
  main, cutoff and divisor-mask sectors to vary with the prime. No entropy,
  mutual-information, short-interval, concentration, or scale-selection theorem
  has been transferred. Such an estimate remains an unresolved premise.

- **Dimitris Koukoulopoulos and James Maynard, _On the Duffin-Schaeffer conjecture_
  (Annals of Mathematics 192 (2020), 251--307),
  DOI 10.4007/annals.2020.192.1.5.**
  Stable source: https://doi.org/10.4007/annals.2020.192.1.5 .
  The paper uses weighted arithmetic overlap analysis together with compression,
  density increment and graph-theoretic structure. The local formal connection is
  only
  `GRWTSK.NumberTheory.Moebius.PickupRoughFarMaskCommutator.divisorMaskCommutator`
  and its exact vanishing theorem
  `divisorMaskCommutator_eq_zero_of_weight_stable`: the moved source
  `pairWeight` differences are now isolated without dropping divisor counts or
  cutoffs. No identification with the Duffin--Schaeffer overlap graph and no
  compression/density-increment inequality is proved locally.

- **Lars Becker, María Inés de Frutos-Fernández, Leo Diedering, Floris van Doorn,
  Sébastien Gouëzel, Evgenia Karunus, Edward van de Meent, Pietro Monticone,
  Jasper Mulder-Sohn, Jim Portegies, Joris Roos, Michael Rothgang, James Sundstrom,
  Jeremy Tan, _Formalizing Carleson's Theorem in Lean_ (2026), arXiv:2609.31334.**
  Stable source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31334 .
  The paper documents a Lean formalization of Carleson's theorem with substantial
  localization and scale-sensitive analytic infrastructure. The local formal
  connection is only the exact selector-change object
  `GRWTSK.NumberTheory.Moebius.PickupRoughFarMaskCommutator.cutoffCommutator`
  and its stability vanishing theorem
  `cutoffCommutator_eq_zero_of_indicator_stable`. No Carleson operator, tile
  estimate, exceptional-set estimate, or theorem from that development is imported.

- **Exact local statements and hypotheses.** The complete frozen far mask is
  factored as a geometric real indicator times the existing `pairWeight`.
  `maskCommutator_eq_cutoff_add_divisor` is a pointwise exact identity for all
  natural parameters and real kernels. For the actual block Gram,
  `fourDifference_actual_farKernel_components` requires the existing primality
  hypotheses for the stage list and inserted prime plus positive source indices.
  `farNegative_eq_transport_components` additionally requires the inserted prime
  to be fresh from the stage list. `farNegative_primeFamily_total` requires every
  member of the finite prime family to be prime and fresh. All original closed
  cutoffs, strict far inequalities, rough-Möbius signs and pair weights remain.
- **Evidence roles.** Imported mathematics: existing GRWTSK/Mathlib source only;
  none from the three papers. Kernel-verified bridges: none claimed. Source proof
  candidates: all declarations above. Conditional interfaces: none hiding a
  cancellation estimate. Finite evidence: none promoted to an asymptotic claim.
  Analogies: exactly the three literature mappings above. Unresolved premises:
  source elaboration, fresh kernel replay, transitive axiom audit, expected
  interfaces, a quantitative bound for the cutoff sector, a quantitative bound for
  the divisor-mask sector, and any valid prime-averaging/entropy/compression bridge.
- **Verification.** Requested source-bound commands are
  `lake build GRWTSK.NumberTheory.ElementaryNumberTheory.ArithmeticFunctionsRelatedNumbersInversionFormulas.PickupRoughFarMaskCommutator`
  and
  `lake env lean --trust=0 -DwarningAsError=true GRWTSK/NumberTheory/ElementaryNumberTheory/ArithmeticFunctionsRelatedNumbersInversionFormulas/PickupRoughFarMaskCommutator.lean`.
  Compilation NOT_RUN; fresh kernel replay NOT_RUN; transitive axiom audit NOT_RUN;
  expected-interface check NOT_RUN. Track the exact source in
  https://github.com/grwtsk/math/issues/1471 .

## Numberphile: repair the zero-exponent remainder proof (2026-10-29)

Local attribution: grwtsk contributors. Actual managed Noe replay of math
`2f23e7a7d53c514789a8d1afb0578e6e40a08004` reached the first source module and
failed at the zero-exponent case of the private `pow_remainder` helper:
`rfl` did not reduce integer powers of zero. The successor replaces that tactic
with `simp only [Int.pow_zero]`, using the exact Lean-core theorem rather than
assuming definitional reduction. No statement, hypothesis, axiom policy,
dependency pin or root import changed.

- **Current source and blob:** `GRWTSK/NumberTheory/SumsOfThreeCubes.lean`,

  `1b87eb53e9d2445a6d46c530f99d040bc5aab6f6`) and
  `GRWTSK/NumberTheory/CastingOutNines.lean` (blob
  `ed5cd7224be30778fb6fa501751bb43ea18f1da5`).
- **Retained actual failure:** Noe runtime
  `formalization/numberphile-r3/run-1/receipt.json`, SHA-256
  `8d3cf15aeff77fb72a19dc2ecea2ec2bc64a145e289c8ff7c536fae8e00f8b21`; `passed=false`, `contracts=[]`,
  `host_config_restored=true`. The source was compiled through the managed
  trust-zero owner. Error-recovery output mentions `sorryAx` in dependent
  declarations after the failed helper; those declarations are rejected, not
  accepted with an exception. Printed empty-axiom reports for some independent
  identities do not turn the failed module into an accepted partial batch.
- **Evidence roles:** the failure is actual source-bound elaboration evidence.
  The repair is a source proof candidate using imported Lean-core arithmetic.
  No newly kernel-verified bridge is claimed yet. Finite identities, conditional
  interfaces, conjectures, video source bylines and missing mathematical bridges

## Numberphile: core-arithmetic successor and casting out nines (2026-10-28)

Local attribution: grwtsk contributors. The fourteen public cube/taxicab theorem
interfaces from math `16d655f2c5662cd3b94e4cbb122298f23343df15` are retained, with
explicit Lean-core proofs replacing the large Mathlib tactic dependency closure.
The original prepared Noe registry had 798 source modules. This is a source-level
proof simplification, not a claim that the original source passed verification.
No dependency version or root import changed. The private `pow_remainder` lemma
uses induction and `Int.mul_emod`; the universal cube result then transports each
integer into one of nine residues. Closed arithmetic proofs use kernel `decide`,
not `native_decide` or an external arithmetic oracle.

- **Successor source pins:**
  `GRWTSK/NumberTheory/DiophantineEquations/SumsOfThreeCubes.lean`, blob
  `d2ce432fa8c0b54e650aa7ea32ce076c0c9e5a03`;
  `GRWTSK/NumberTheory/DiophantineEquations/Taxicab.lean`, blob
  `1b87eb53e9d2445a6d46c530f99d040bc5aab6f6`.
  Their source titles, public bylines, dates, hypotheses, fourteen fully qualified
  public theorem names, explicit witnesses and mathematical limitations are those
  recorded in the first batch below. The containing publication commit pins the
  complete successor tree; earlier blob identities remain historical evidence.
- **New primary source:** Numberphile, *Casting Out Nines*, video by Brady Haran,
  featuring James Grime, 2017-09-10,
  https://www.numberphile.com/videos/casting-out-nines ;
  original video https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=FlndIiQa20o . The official page
  describes checking calculations. This entry formalizes the elementary method
  and its mathematical limits; complete timestamped video claim extraction is
  NOT_DONE. No claim is made that the video's exact examples are the local examples.
- **New source path/blob:**
  `GRWTSK/NumberTheory/ElementaryNumberTheory/CastingOutNines.lean`, blob
  `ed5cd7224be30778fb6fa501751bb43ea18f1da5`; containing publication commit pins
  the full source. Definitions `value` and `digitSum` take finite natural-number
  coefficient lists, least significant first. Coefficients below ten give ordinary
  decimal strings; the stronger congruence also holds for unrestricted natural
  coefficients, the empty list, zero and leading zeroes.
- **Exact method and consumer:** induction proves `value ds % 9 = digitSum ds % 9`.
  `AddCheck` and `MulCheck` compare reduced digit sums. They are equivalent to
  equality of numerical remainders, so a failed check certifies a wrong addition
  or multiplication. The converses are false: the incorrect answers `1+1=11` and
  `2*2=13` pass. For every natural `n,k`, the distinct number `n+9*(k+1)` has the
  same remainder as `n`. Swapping adjacent digits at any position, with arbitrary
  prefix and suffix, also preserves the checksum. These universal statements
  explain both the usefulness and the exact limitation of the checking method.
- **Fully qualified new declarations:** prefix
  `GRWTSK.NumberTheory.ElementaryNumberTheory.CastingOutNines.` with definitions
  `value`, `digitSum`, `AddCheck`, `MulCheck` and theorems `casting_out_nines`,
  `divisible_by_nine_iff`, `addCheck_iff`, `mulCheck_iff`,
  `correct_addition_passes`, `correct_multiplication_passes`,
  `failed_addition_check_detects_error`, `failed_multiplication_check_detects_error`,
  `addition_false_positive`, `multiplication_false_positive`,
  `undetectable_error_family`, `adjacent_transposition_undetectable`.
- **Evidence roles:** imported mathematics is Lean-core integer/natural arithmetic,
  finite lists and the proof-producing omega tactic. Kernel-verified bridges:
  none claimed at publication. Source proof candidates: the fourteen retained
  public results and twelve new public results, plus the transitive private
  remainder helper. Conditional interfaces: correctness hypotheses are explicit
  only in the two forward checking implications, and contraposition detects errors.
  Finite evidence: explicit counterexamples and cube identities. Analogies: none.
  Open premises: source elaboration, source-bound kernel/type checks and complete
  transitive axiom reports; three-cubes sufficiency and taxicab minimality remain
  unproved, and no entire video is marked complete.
- **Actual Noe attempt at the original source:** managed registry v3 preparation
  succeeded for all fourteen original theorem names at math `16d655f2c5662cd3b94e4cbb122298f23343df15`.
  Two one-shot launchd starts observed exit 78 before a supervisor start record;
  no replay-child result or Lean execution is inferred from them. The documented
  direct replay command then stopped with `BlockingIOError: [Errno 35] Resource
  temporarily unavailable` at the exclusive activation lock, held by the existing
  3Blue1Brown replay. Requests, registries, stderr, and the blocked-attempt summary
  are retained externally in Noe runtime `formalization/numberphile-r1/`.
  This is blocked admission, not a failed mathematical proof. No lock, reservation,
  host configuration or resource policy was cleared or changed by this request.
- **Verification status:** compilation NOT_RUN; fresh kernel checking NOT_RUN;
  transitive axiom auditing NOT_RUN for both the original and successor sources.
  The next managed request must pin the successor publication commit, all three
  source blobs, the current Noe producer, Lean toolchain and dependency lock. Audit
  all twenty-six public theorem declarations and their transitive dependencies;
  retain actual failures before source fixes. Track exact evidence and next steps
  in https://github.com/grwtsk/math/issues/1440 .

## Numberphile: first sums-of-cubes claim batch (2026-10-28)

Local attribution: grwtsk contributors. This is a partial claim formalization,
not completion of five videos or the entire channel. The passive scope manifest
`documentation/mathematics/_verification/specifications/_unscoped/numberphile-corpus-v1.json`
records 1,165 distinct official sitemap video-page URLs, the raw sitemap digest,
five partial claim mappings and explicit acceptance requirements. Descriptions
were consulted; complete video/transcript claim extraction remains NOT_DONE.
The sitemap is not a complete YouTube-channel inventory. No historical influence
or novelty is inferred from a topic match.

- **Primary video sources/bylines/versions:** Numberphile, videos by Brady Haran:
  *The Uncracked Problem with 33*, featuring Tim Browning (2015-11-06),
  https://www.numberphile.com/videos/the-uncracked-problem-with-33 ;
  *42 is the new 33*, featuring Andrew Booker (2019-03-11),
  https://www.numberphile.com/videos/42-is-the-new-33 ;
  *The Mystery of 42 is Solved*, featuring Andrew Booker (2019-09-06),
  https://www.numberphile.com/videos/the-mystery-of-42-is-solved ;
  *3 as the sum of the 3 cubes*, featuring Andrew Booker (2019-09-24),
  https://www.numberphile.com/videos/3-as-the-sum-of-the-3-cubes ;
  *1729 and Taxi Cabs*, featuring James Grime and Roger Bowley (2012-02-28),
  https://www.numberphile.com/videos/1729-and-taxi-cabs . Dates are the official
  pages' publication metadata. These are video-derived elementary claims, not
  imported proofs from papers linked in the descriptions.
- **Independent primary witness source:** Andrew V. Sutherland, *Sums of cubes*,
  https://math.mit.edu/~drew/sumsofcubes.html (joint results with Andrew R. Booker;
  mutable page consulted 2026-10-28, no immutable upstream Git identity claimed).
  The large witness for three and the witness for 42 are joint results. The 33
  identity is also given in the original video's March 2019 description update.
  The historical descriptions of 33 and 42 as unsolved are not current premises.
- **Exact integer statement:** `Representable n` means there exist unrestricted
  integers `x,y,z` with `x^3+y^3+z^3=n`. Every integer cube has remainder 0, 1 or 8
  modulo 9; hence a representable integer has neither remainder 4 nor remainder 5.
  The reduction modulo nine is universal, including negative integers. The
  unrestricted sufficiency assertion is only the proposition definition
  `Conjecture`; it is neither an axiom nor a proved implication here.
- **Explicit identities:** the witnesses are
  `(8866128975287528,-8778405442862239,-2736111468807040)` for 33,
  `(-80538738812075974,80435758145817515,12602123297335631)` for 42, and
  `(569936821221962380720,-569936821113563493509,-472715493453327032)` for 3.
  Two smaller triples for 3 are `(1,1,1)` and `(4,4,-5)`; their first coordinates
  distinguish all three displayed ordered triples. This is not completeness,
  optimality, a computation-time claim or an unbounded search theorem.
- **Exact taxicab statement:** positive natural pairs `(1,12)` and `(9,10)` are
  individually increasing and different, and both sums of cubes equal 1729.
  `HasTwoRepresentations` excludes duplicates caused solely by swapping a pair.
  No theorem here states that 1729 is the least such integer.
- **Local paths/blobs:**
  `GRWTSK/NumberTheory/DiophantineEquations/SumsOfThreeCubes.lean`,
  blob `9dc4da6fb7cd3ae134bf5cddc73256d66a623bf3`;
  `GRWTSK/NumberTheory/DiophantineEquations/Taxicab.lean`,
  blob `d2baea4abbb3dd2581fb653d5a44d925539bfcaa`.
  The containing publication commit supplies the complete source tree.
- **Fully qualified declarations:** prefix
  `GRWTSK.NumberTheory.DiophantineEquations.SumsOfThreeCubes.` with
  `Representable`, `Admissible`, `Conjecture`, `cube_residue`,
  `representable_admissible`, `not_representable_of_residue_four`,
  `not_representable_of_residue_five`, `identity_33`, `representable_33`,
  `identity_42`, `representable_42`, `identity_3_large`, `representable_3_large`,
  `three_distinct_solutions_for_3`; prefix
  `GRWTSK.NumberTheory.DiophantineEquations.Taxicab.` with
  `HasTwoRepresentations`, `identity_1729_first`, `identity_1729_second`,
  `two_representations_1729`.
- **Evidence roles:** imported mathematics is Mathlib integer congruence and
  proof-producing arithmetic tactics under the existing dependency pins. New
  kernel-verified bridges: none claimed. Source proof candidates: the fourteen
  named theorems. Conditional interfaces: no new hypotheses disguising an

### Problem #138: transport an actual upstream proof to the positive-interval definition

- **Source title/byline/version:** OpenAI, *Quantitative Superexponential Bounds for van der Waerden Numbers*, September 23, 2026, pinned `openai/math` commit `7ef5c0cc5ca4b0e072b7a050fca09ab24bc6fa3a`. Stable source: https://github.com/openai/math/blob/7ef5c0cc5ca4b0e072b7a050fca09ab24bc6fa3a/preprints/Quantitative-Superexponential-Bounds-for-van-der-Waerden-Numbers-September-23-2026/paper.pdf . - **Exact imported theorem:** `OAI.QuantitativeVanDerWaerden.kthRoot_tendsto`, for each fixed natural color count `r ≥ 2`, asserts `Tendsto (fun k : ℕ => (W r k : ℝ) ^ (1 / (k : ℝ))) atTop atTop`. Its proof body in `lean/OAI/Combinatorics/ProgressionColoring/Main.lean` invokes the cyclic-coloring construction and its growth theorem. Shifting a coloring by one proves equivalence with upstream's zero-based interval; the sets of admissible positive `N` and their infima are equal. The final theorem specializes the actual upstream limit to `r=2`. No analytic estimate is hidden in a new premise.

### Problem #242: multiplicative transport and a residue-one-modulo-24 reduction

- **Source statement/byline/version:** Thomas Bloom, *Erdős Problem #242*, https://www.erdosproblems.com/latex/242, consulted 2026-10-28; mutable catalogue, with no immutable web blob claimed. Exact statement and earlier families are recorded below.

- **Exact witnesses/proof:** any representation scales by a positive integer `k`, multiplying all three ordered denominators by `k`. For `n=3k+2`, `k≥1`, use `(k+1,n,n(k+1))`; for `n=8k+5`, `k≥0`, use `(2(k+1),n(k+1),2n(k+1))`. Multiples of three inherit the representation `(1,4,12)` of three. Together with the earlier even and `3 mod 4` families, natural-number residue arithmetic leaves only `n ≡ 1 mod 24`. These are alternative constructive routes; the residual universal statement remains a necessary joint premise for concluding the full conjecture.
- **Roles/verification:** source proof candidates only; imported mathematics is Mathlib arithmetic. Kernel-verified bridges: none claimed; finite evidence and analogies: none. The remaining residue class is not proved by this reduction.

### Problem #1: count distinct subset sums before seeking a stronger estimate

- **Source/byline/version:** Thomas Bloom, *Erdős Problem #1*, https://www.erdosproblems.com/1, consulted 2026-10-28; mutable catalogue with no immutable web blob claimed. The source states the elementary `2^n/n` counting lower bound. The stronger proposed absolute-constant exponential bound is kept as a proposition definition, with no claim about its current external resolution status.
- **Exact hypotheses/consumer:** `A : Finset ℕ`, all subset sums distinct including the empty sum; if every element is at most `N`, then `2^A.card ≤ A.card*N+1`. The full statement additionally requires nonempty `A` and positive elements, and quantifies one positive real constant independently of both `A` and `N`. Nonemptiness prevents the false empty-set, `N=0` boundary case. The partial counting theorem itself needs neither positivity nor nonemptiness.
- **Proof route:** inject the powerset into `range (sum A + 1)` using subset summation, giving `2^A.card ≤ sum A + 1`; then bound the total sum by `A.card*N`. The missing factor `A.card` cannot be deleted by this argument. A stronger concentration or additive-structure estimate is an additional mathematical obligation, not a premise concealed in a wrapper.
- **Local path/blob/declarations:** `GRWTSK/NumberTheory/AdditiveNumberTheory/DistinctSubsetSums.lean`, blob `4128530ca26874505217aeab1758b383137d68ea`; namespace `GRWTSK.NumberTheory.AdditiveNumberTheory.DistinctSubsetSums` with `SumDistinct`, `Conjecture`, `pow_card_le_total_sum_add_one`, `pow_card_le_card_mul_bound_add_one`.
- **Roles/verification:** imported mathematics is Mathlib finite powersets, cardinality of an injective image, and monotonicity of natural-number sums. New source proof candidates are the two counting inequalities. Kernel-verified bridges: none claimed; conditional interfaces, finite computation and analogies: none used. Local compilation, fresh kernel replay and both axiom audits are `NOT_RUN`, pending supported Noe toolchain admission. Intended command: `lake env lean GRWTSK/NumberTheory/AdditiveNumberTheory/DistinctSubsetSums.lean` through Noe at the publication commit. This is not a proof of the full absolute-constant statement.

## Erdős Problem #242: distinct-denominator Erdős–Straus representations (first increment)

- **Source/byline/version:** Thomas Bloom, *Erdős Problem #242*, catalogue statement consulted 2026-10-28; https://www.erdosproblems.com/latex/242 and https://www.erdosproblems.com/242. Problem attributed to Paul Erdős and Ernst G. Straus. This entry imports the catalogue statement, not a paper's proof or a claim of novelty. The web statement is mutable; no immutable upstream commit/blob is available for this consultation.
- **Exact statement:** for every natural integer `n > 2`, there are natural integers `0 < x < y < z` with `4/n = 1/x + 1/y + 1/z` in the rationals. Strict distinctness and the lower bound on `n` are retained.
- **Local source:** `GRWTSK/NumberTheory/DiophantineEquations/ErdosStraus.lean`, blob `cd3f50c44df66fb49d11b5a28509b99cc877c2ed`; base math commit `ad2ef5597215827580d7c48ef0b81fcf92996cb0`. - **Exact elementary methods/hypotheses:** for `n = 2k`, `k ≥ 2`, use `(k, k+1, k(k+1))`; for `n = 4k+3`, `k ≥ 0`, put `a = (4k+3)(k+1)` and use `(k+1, a+1, a(a+1))`. Positivity, strict ordering, and the rational identities have source proof bodies. These families cover all `n > 2` except residue one modulo four. Their consumer is the equivalence reducing `Conjecture` to that remaining residue class.
- **Imported mathematics:** Mathlib rational arithmetic, natural division/remainders, ordered arithmetic and tactics, under the existing repository dependencies; no upstream Erdős–Straus proof is imported.
- **Kernel-verified bridges:** none claimed. **Source proof candidates:** the five named theorems above. **Conditional interfaces:** the reverse direction of `conjecture_iff_residue_one` requires representations for every `n > 2` congruent to one modulo four. **Finite evidence:** none used. **Analogies:** none asserted.
- **Unresolved premises/bridges:** the entire remaining residue class; source elaboration, fresh kernel checking, and transitive axiom audit. `Conjecture` is a definition of a proposition, with no axiom, `sorry`, or asserted general solution. This increment does not resolve Problem #242.

## OpenAI (2026): QAOA attains the SK ground-state energy in the thermodynamic-first limit

- **Upstream title/byline/version:** *QAOA attains the SK ground-state energy in the thermodynamic-first limit*; OpenAI; September 25, 2026 preprint.
- **Stable source:** https://github.com/openai/math/blob/1581d4cbdd69a0faf34b2089614fe2a814316a6a/preprints/QAOA-attains-the-SK-ground-state-energy-in-the-thermodynamic-first-limit-September-25-2026/QAOA-attains-the-SK-ground-state-energy-in-the-thermodynamic-first-limit-September-25-2026.pdf
- **Upstream immutable identity:** `openai/math` commit `1581d4cbdd69a0faf34b2089614fe2a814316a6a`; theorem-statement source `preprints/QAOA-attains-the-SK-ground-state-energy-in-the-thermodynamic-first-limit-September-25-2026/build/sections/01-introduction.tex`, blob `453caa0efc98c73518eb85f3c05bf03743b22e8b`.
- **Source results and exact limitations:** The manuscript's `thm:main` asserts `lim_{p→∞} Q_p = P_*` for zero-field Gaussian Sherrington–Kirkpatrick spins with Hamiltonian `h_{n,J}(σ) = n^{-1/2} Σ_{i<j} J_{ij} σ_i σ_j`, iid standard Gaussian `J_{ij}`, and deterministic fixed QAOA angles selected independently of `n` and `J` before the thermodynamic limit. Its `cor:regular-maxcut` concerns fixed-depth circuit expectations on sufficiently high-girth `(D+1)`-regular graphs (`girth > 2p+1`) and successive random-regular limits, with cost angles `2γ/√D`; it is not an all-graph optimality claim.
- **Method used locally:** Only the **finite classical** relation between an Ising `±1` spin difference, an edge-cut indicator, and the oriented squared-difference energy. The manuscript's MaxCut cost is `H_MC(G) = 1/2 Σ_{\{u,v\}∈E}(I-Z_u Z_v)`; no QAOA, Gaussian or Parisi theorem is imported as a proved local dependency. The elementary factor-eight calculation is a source-level bridge proposed here, not an upstream Lean theorem transported into this repository.
- **Exact local carrier hypotheses:** `SimpleEdgePresentation V` has a finite set of ordered endpoint pairs; no representative has equal endpoints; no pair appears together with its reversal. Thus each edge of the *represented finite simple undirected graph* has one representative. A spin assignment is any function `σ : V → Bool`, interpreted as `±1`. `cutSize` sums one zero/one indicator per representative; `orientedEnergy` sums the two squared spin differences per representative. It does **not** yet identify the carrier with an arbitrary `SimpleGraph` or the archived spectral `CellGraph`.
- **Local fully qualified source declarations:**
  - `GRWTSK.CombinatoricsAndDiscreteMathematics.GraphTheory.SpinCutEnergy.SimpleEdgePresentation`
  - `GRWTSK.CombinatoricsAndDiscreteMathematics.GraphTheory.SpinCutEnergy.spin`
  - `GRWTSK.CombinatoricsAndDiscreteMathematics.GraphTheory.SpinCutEnergy.cutIndicator`
  - `GRWTSK.CombinatoricsAndDiscreteMathematics.GraphTheory.SpinCutEnergy.cutSize`
  - `GRWTSK.CombinatoricsAndDiscreteMathematics.GraphTheory.SpinCutEnergy.edgeEnergy`
  - `GRWTSK.CombinatoricsAndDiscreteMathematics.GraphTheory.SpinCutEnergy.orientedEnergy`

  - `GRWTSK.CombinatoricsAndDiscreteMathematics.GraphTheory.SpinCutEnergy.spin_oriented_difference_sq`

  - `GRWTSK.CombinatoricsAndDiscreteMathematics.GraphTheory.SpinCutEnergy.orientedEnergy_eq_eight_cutSize`
- **Local source path and pin:** `GRWTSK/CombinatoricsAndDiscreteMathematics/GraphTheory/SpinCutEnergy.lean`; source commit `abcc289a8d926dab071031ecef7225bd6e3aa86b`; blob `e1fab09ea5cb7c53057467aab11f82db6016e9bd`.

### Finite diagonal MaxCut Hamiltonian and Born expectation (second bounded increment)

- **Upstream method and provenance:** OpenAI, *QAOA attains the SK ground-state energy in the thermodynamic-first limit*, September 25, 2026; immutable preprint source and upstream commit are given above. In the manuscript's `sec:regular-maxcut`, the finite graph cost Hamiltonian is `H_MC(G) = (1/2) Σ_{\{u,v\}∈E}(I-Z_u Z_v)`, with computational-basis measurement producing cut values. The **finite diagonal** eigenvalue/expectation identity is the only piece addressed in this new increment; the full QAOA theorem and the high-girth corollary are **not** imported or claimed.
- **Exact local hypotheses:** A finite vertex type `V` with `[Fintype V]` and `[DecidableEq V]`; `G : SimpleEdgePresentation V` whose `Finset (V × V)` contains exactly one ordered representative of each represented undirected simple edge, no loops and no reverse duplicates; `Configuration V = V → Bool`; `ψ : Configuration V → ℂ`. A computational basis state is the indicator vector for a single configuration. `IsNormalized ψ` means `∑ σ, Complex.normSq (ψ σ) = 1` and is required **only** for the maximum-cut upper bound.
- **Corresponding finite formulas:** `pauliZEdgeCost a b = (1 - spin a * spin b)/2`, which equals the Boolean cut indicator. `diagonalHamiltonian G` is a complex-linear diagonal operator with eigenvalue `cutSize G σ` on each configuration `σ`. `hamiltonianExpectation G ψ = ∑ σ, re (conj (ψ σ) * (diagonalHamiltonian G ψ) σ)` equals `bornCutAverage G ψ = ∑ σ, Complex.normSq (ψ σ) * cutSize G σ`, also equals `(1/8)∑ σ, Complex.normSq (ψ σ) * orientedEnergy G σ`. `maxCut G` is the maximum over the finite set of all classical spin assignments; normalized states satisfy `0 ≤ hamiltonianExpectation G ψ ≤ maxCut G`.

### Finite cost/mixer actions and off-support QAOA gate commutation (third bounded increment)

- **Paper and pinned provenance:** OpenAI (2026), *QAOA attains the SK ground-state energy in the thermodynamic-first limit*, September 25, 2026. Immutable upstream paper and \`openai/math\` commit \`1581d4cbdd69a0faf34b2089614fe2a814316a6a\` are identified above. Relevant methods are the finite MaxCut cost \`H_MC(G)=1/2 Σ_{ {u,v}∈E }(I-Z_u Z_v)\`, the cost/mixer circuit in the upstream \`01-introduction.tex\` (blob \`453caa0efc98c73518eb85f3c05bf03743b22e8b\`), and finite-word locality prerequisites in \`02-foundations.tex\` (blob \`5754be6cad8d5b840de87906148835e8e666f32b\`). This local increment independently proves only elementary finite gate commutation, not those later theorems. Neither title nor mathematical similarity implies historical influence.
- **Exact local carrier and limits:** A finite vertex type \`V\` with \`[Fintype V] [DecidableEq V]\`, \`G : SpinCutEnergy.SimpleEdgePresentation V\` (one oriented representative per simple undirected edge, no loops or reverse duplicate), computational basis \`Configuration V := V → Bool\`, complex state \`State V := Configuration V → ℂ\`, real angles \`γ, β\`, and an arbitrary \`ψ : State V\`. The cost layer multiplies each amplitude at \`σ\` by \`Complex.exp (-Complex.I * (γ:ℂ) * (cutSize G σ:ℂ))\`. Each site mixer acts by \`(cos β:ℂ) ψ σ - Complex.I * (sin β:ℂ) ψ (flipAt v σ)\`. \`mixerList\` retains the exact stated site order; \`mixerLayer\` chooses \`Finset.univ.toList\`; \`qaoaCircuit\` folds over a finite list of angle pairs, first pair first. These *definitions* are not yet proofs of normality, gate unitarity, order independence or an exponential-of-total-mixer identity.
- **Locality hypotheses:** \`IsLocalDiagonal S f\` means \`f σ=f τ\` whenever \`σ,τ\` agree on *every* site of the finite \`S\`. When \`v∉S\`, \`f (flipAt v σ)=f σ\`; hence the one-site mixer commutes with \`diagonal f\`. The list theorem assumes that **every vertex in the supplied list lies outside** \`S\`; no unprovided independence, disjointness between list entries, or unit-norm condition is silently assumed. For edge \`e=(u,v)\`, the edge symbol and phase depend only on \`{u,v}\`; the corresponding off-edge mixer hypotheses are \`w≠u\` and \`w≠v\`.

### Non-diagonal finite QAOA operator-kernel support and one-edge Heisenberg conjugation (fifth bounded increment)

- **Upstream paper/title/byline/version:** OpenAI, *QAOA attains the SK ground-state energy in the thermodynamic-first limit*, September 25, 2026 preprint. Method used: finite cost-gate factorization, locality of a cost edge under Heisenberg conjugation, and eventual restriction of fixed-depth edge observables to causal cones. The asymptotic QAOA theorem and its tree/SK transfer are **not** imported or proved by the present increment.
- **Exact finite hypotheses:** A finite vertex type `V` with `[Fintype V]` and `[DecidableEq V]`, Boolean configurations `Configuration V := V → Bool`, and an arbitrary complex matrix kernel `K : (Configuration V → Configuration V → ℂ)`. A declared support `S : Finset V` satisfies **both**: (1) `K σ τ=0` whenever `σ v≠τ v` for any `v∉S`; (2) `K (flipAt v σ) (flipAt v τ)=K σ τ` for any `v∉S`. These are the explicit local tensor-factor entry conditions; the second condition prevents hidden dependence on external spin labels. They do not silently assume diagonal operators, normalization, self-adjointness, or a pre-existing abstract operator-support API.
- **Composition/source theorem:** Define `kernelComposition K L σ τ := ∑ρ, K σ ρ * L ρ τ` over the **entire** finite configuration carrier. If `K` and `L` have supports `S` and `T` respectively, the composition is supported on `S∪T`. Proof candidate uses pointwise vanishing for off-support transitions and reindexing the finite complex sum by the spin-flip involution for simultaneous-outside-flip invariance. This statement is finite matrix-kernel multiplication, not a physical locality bound for an arbitrary previously undefined quantum operator.
- **Single-edge conjugation/source theorem:** For arbitrary real `γ` and an edge-endpoint pair `e : V × V`, `edgePhaseConjugate γ e K σ τ := Complex.conj (edgePhase γ e σ) * K σ τ * edgePhase γ e τ`. If `K` is `S`-local, this entrywise `U_e† K U_e` kernel is `S ∪ {e.1,e.2}`-local. If `e.1∉S` and `e.2∉S`, then **exact equality of kernels** `edgePhaseConjugate γ e K = K` follows using the off-support zero condition and the unit-modulus edge phase. The cost edge is the one-count-per-undirected-edge MaxCut edge phase of the existing `QAOAFiniteLocality` representation, not an oriented-energy factor-eight phase.

### Finite Pauli-X mixer kernel and exact non-diagonal Heisenberg support (sixth bounded increment)

- **Upstream source, exact scope, authorship:** OpenAI, *QAOA attains the SK ground-state energy in the thermodynamic-first limit*, September 25, 2026 preprint, upstream `openai/math` commit `1581d4cbdd69a0faf34b2089614fe2a814316a6a`. No historical influence inferred from matching expressions.

No diagonal, self-adjoint, normalized-state, graph-degree, or girth hypothesis is inserted.
- **Single-site mixer operator:** `mixerKernel β v σ τ` is the complex matrix `cos(β) 1[σ=τ] - i sin(β) 1[σ=flipAt v τ]`. Its source theorem `mixerKernel_local` states `IsLocalKernel {v} (mixerKernel β v)`. `kernelAction` is the entire finite matrix-vector sum. The named source theorem `mixerKernel_action_eq_mixerAt` identifies that matrix action with the **already defined** amplitude-level `QAOAFiniteLocality.mixerAt`, fixing sign and gate ordering without assuming an arbitrary supplied mixer.
- **Physical Heisenberg interpretation and exact algebra:** Source-level `mixerKernel_symmetric` and `mixerKernel_conj_transpose` identify the reverse-angle kernel `mixerKernel (-β) v` with the conjugate transpose of `mixerKernel β v`. The precise two-sided kernel product `mixerKernelConjugation β v K := kernelComposition (mixerKernel (-β) v) (kernelComposition K (mixerKernel β v))` is rewritten by `mixerKernelConjugation_eq_polynomial` as `cos²(β) K + sin²(β) X_v K X_v + i cos(β) sin(β)(X_v K - K X_v)`, expressed entrywise for all `σ,τ`. The proof uses *actual* finite kernel multiplication; no unproved identification with the polynomial is assumed in the theorem statement.
- **Bounded support conclusions:** `mixerHeisenbergPolynomial_local` and `mixerKernelConjugation_local` assert that a mixer at **any** site preserves the *same* `S` support for any `IsLocalKernel S K`, even if `K` is non-diagonal. When additionally `v∉S`, `flip_row_eq_flip_column_of_outside` and `mixerKernelConjugation_eq_of_outside` assert exact cancellation, i.e. the whole conjugated kernel equals `K`. The proof uses `Real.cos_sq_add_sin_sq`, the existing tensor-locality predicate, and site-flip commutation. These are fixed, finite algebraic source-level statements, not a global depth-p QAOA cone.

### Whole-graph finite QAOA cost-layer Heisenberg locality (seventh bounded increment)

- **Upstream research:** OpenAI, *QAOA attains the SK ground-state energy in the thermodynamic-first limit*, OpenAI, September 25, 2026 preprint. Stable paper URL and source theorem statement with exact hypotheses appear at the beginning of this QAOA section; upstream immutable repository commit `1581d4cbdd69a0faf34b2089614fe2a814316a6a`. The imported *method*, not a proved upstream Lean dependency, is the one-count-per-undirected-edge MaxCut phase `exp(-iγ C_G)` and the fixed-word local tree cone. No historical influence is inferred from mathematical similarity. No SK/Parisi convergence, seeded-control synthesis, or QAOA circuit limit is claimed by this finite source.
- **Exact mathematical carrier/hypotheses:** Finite type `V` with `[Fintype V] [DecidableEq V]`; `G : SpinCutEnergy.SimpleEdgePresentation V` with `G.edges : Finset (V × V)` containing one oriented representative per underlying simple undirected edge, with no loops or reversed duplicate representatives; Boolean configuration `Configuration V := V→Bool`, complex amplitude `State V`, arbitrary complex operator kernel `K : Kernel V`, real phase angle `γ`, finite observable support `S : Finset V`, and the fully specified `QAOAOperatorSupport.IsLocalKernel S K` certificate, comprising (i) vanishing matrix entries when row/column spins disagree outside S, and (ii) invariance under simultaneous row/column spin flips outside S. There is NO degree, girth, randomness, normalized-state, self-adjointness, or Gaussian-limit hypothesis.
- **Exact partition and cost normalization:** `touches S e` iff `e.1∈S ∨ e.2∈S`. `incidentEdges G S := G.edges.filter (touches S)`; `remoteEdges G S := G.edges.filter (fun e => ¬touches S e)`. Each edge is counted exactly once in `incidentCost` or `remoteCost` using `QuantumMaxCut.pauliZEdgeCost`, so `incidentCost G S σ + remoteCost G S σ = SpinCutEnergy.cutSize G σ`, *not* eight times a cut. Their cost phases multiply to `fullCostPhase G γ σ = exp(-i γ cutSize G σ)` with no global-phase slack. The established upstream local definition `QAOAFiniteLocality.incidentClosure G S` includes S and both endpoints of **edges originally touching S**; this is the single spatial expansion counted by the theorem.
- **Exact mathematical step:** If `K σ τ ≠ 0` for an S-local kernel, the spin configurations σ and τ coincide at every endpoint of a remote edge. Thus the remote cost/phase agree between σ and τ and its complex conjugate times itself equals one. The full graph conjugation `fullCostConjugate G γ K σ τ := conj(fullCostPhase G γ σ) * K σ τ * fullCostPhase G γ τ` equals `incidentCostConjugate G γ S K` **on every kernel entry**, not merely in expectation. The incident phase is local to `incidentClosure G S`, proving `IsLocalKernel (incidentClosure G S) (fullCostConjugate G γ K)`. This is non-diagonal operator-kernel support for one full *cost* layer, not a depth-p QAOA circuit bound.
- **Connection to the actual finite operator:** `costLayer_apply_fullCostPhase` and `fullCostPhase_eq_edgePhase_product` identify the full scalar cost with the existing QAOA amplitude gate and the product of its one-edge factors. `costKernel` is `diagonalKernel (fullCostPhase G γ)`; `costKernel_action_eq_costLayer` identifies finite matrix-vector application. `kernelComposition_diagonal_left` and `kernelComposition_diagonal_right` identify diagonal matrix multiplication. `fullCostConjugate_eq_kernelComposition` identifies the full two-sided matrix-kernel product with the conjugate-phase diagonal and the cost kernel. No assertion of a general full-QAOA unitary, graph tree equivalence, or thermodynamic limit is inserted.

The existing `kernelComposition A B σ τ := ∑ρ A σ ρ * B ρ τ`, `kernelAction U ψ σ := ∑τ U σ τ * ψ τ`, and finite configuration carrier are reused, not replaced by a parallel matrix theory. Source lemmas prove identity action, associativity of finite matrix multiplication, matrix-vector composition, reversal of adjoints, and anti-homomorphic conjugation of composed forward kernels.
- **Exact forward gate order:** `mixerListKernel β [] = identityKernel` and `mixerListKernel β (v::vs) = kernelComposition (mixerKernel β v) (mixerListKernel β vs)`; its action is **identically** the existing `QAOAFiniteLocality.mixerList β vs`, allowing repeated vertices and arbitrary list order. `mixerLayerKernel β := mixerListKernel β (Finset.univ.toList : List V)`, literally the pre-existing full-site mixer order, without an unproved commutativity/order-independence premise. `qaoaRoundKernel G γ β := kernelComposition (mixerLayerKernel β) (QAOACostLayerCone.costKernel G γ)`, so cost acts first on amplitudes, mixer second. `qaoaCircuitKernel G angles := angles.foldl (fun U pair => kernelComposition (qaoaRoundKernel G pair.1 pair.2) U) identityKernel`: the FIRST pair acts FIRST, later rounds multiply on the LEFT. This matches exactly the earlier amplitude `qaoaCircuit` semantics.
- **Exact forward representation theorem:** `qaoaCircuitKernel_action_eq_qaoaCircuit` states `kernelAction (qaoaCircuitKernel G angles) ψ = qaoaCircuit G angles ψ` **for every** complex state, without assuming normalized input. Proof candidate is a finite `foldl` accumulator induction using the actual matrix-action identities of mixer and cost kernels, not a convenient substitute circuit.
- **Exact backward representation theorem:** `qaoaCircuitKernel_conjugate_eq_heisenberg` states `kernelConjugate (qaoaCircuitKernel G angles) K = qaoaCircuitHeisenberg G angles K` for **every** complex operator kernel, including non-diagonal. The construction uses `mixerKernel_conj_transpose` from the imported QAOA mixer source, the real cost phase conjugate, conjugate-transpose reversal of finite matrix products, the exact mixer-sweep order, and an independent finite `foldl` conjugation induction. Thus the source states a genuine full-circuit adjoint/product equality rather than only the previously defined gatewise recurrence; no circular assumption equates them.

### Finite QAOA cone to Mathlib adjacency and strict-girth bridge (tenth bounded increment)

- **Upstream title, byline, year/version and identifier:** OpenAI, *QAOA attains the SK ground-state energy in the thermodynamic-first limit*, September 25, 2026 preprint; upstream immutable repository `openai/math` at commit `1581d4cbdd69a0faf34b2089614fe2a814316a6a`. Stable manuscript source: https://github.com/openai/math/tree/1581d4cbdd69a0faf34b2089614fe2a814316a6a/preprints/QAOA-attains-the-SK-ground-state-energy-in-the-thermodynamic-first-limit-September-25-2026 . Exact theorem-statement source `build/sections/01-introduction.tex` Git blob `453caa0efc98c73518eb85f3c05bf03743b22e8b`. **Method/hypotheses actually used:** the high-girth regular-MaxCut corollary assumes fixed finite QAOA depth p and simple (D+1)-regular graph with `girth > 2p+1`, and comparisons depend on a graph-theoretic causal cone. The paper's QAOA/Parisi asymptotics are not an imported Lean proof; local source uses only a finite graph carrier and Mathlib girth/adjacency.
- **Exact graph representation and assumptions:** `[Fintype V] [DecidableEq V]`, `G : GRWTSK.CombinatoricsAndDiscreteMathematics.GraphTheory.SpinCutEnergy.SimpleEdgePresentation V`. Thus `G.edges : Finset (V × V)` gives exactly one chosen oriented representative per represented undirected edge, with `loopless` and `noReverse` fields. Local `presentedAdj G u v` is defined by `(u,v)∈G.edges ∨ (v,u)∈G.edges`; `presentedGraph G : SimpleGraph V` uses this adjacency and separately proves symmetry and irreflexivity. **No new graph edge is silently added** and no global orientation is claimed physical.
- **QAOA graph-neighborhood bridge:** `graphNeighborhood G S := S ∪ Finset.univ.filter (fun w => ∃ u∈S, (presentedGraph G).Adj u w)` (classical finite definition). The source theorem `mem_incidentClosure_iff` proves exact membership equivalence between the earlier finite-QAOA `incidentClosure G S` and either `w∈S` or adjacency to a vertex of S. `incidentClosure_eq_graphNeighborhood` establishes literal equality of these two `Finset V`s. `graphCone G S p` is exactly p iterations of the Mathlib-adjacency neighborhood; `cone_eq_graphCone` proves equality with the original QAOA cone for every p. This identifies the existing causal support in the Mathlib graph carrier, rather than assuming it from an analogy.
- **Exact quantum-support consequence:** From the previously declared `QAOACircuitMatrix.qaoaCircuitKernel_edge_cone`, `qaoaCircuitKernel_edge_graphCone` states `IsLocalKernel (graphCone G {e.1,e.2} angles.length) (kernelConjugate (qaoaCircuitKernel G angles) (edgeObservableKernel e))`, for arbitrary finite angle list and endpoint pair. Original theorem/regular-MaxCut method: for fixed depth p, backwards cancellation of cost gates retains only cost edges having an endpoint at distance at most p−1 from the two endpoints of the distinguished edge; an extra edge in the relevant tree cone would close a cycle of length at most 2p+1. The manuscript uses `girth > 2p+1` and (D+1)-regularity in a subsequent regular-tree comparison. **Only the finite pruning/cost-edge counting method is newly formalized here**. Membership is equivalent to **both** `e∈G.edges` and `touches (cone G S n) e`, i.e., at least one endpoint belongs to the ORIGINAL radius-n cone. Hence at depth p>0, relevant cost edges touch the original radius-(p−1) cone; it is **not** the set of all edges induced by the radius-p vertex cone. Every relevant edge lies in the graph and has both endpoints in radius p, but the reverse implication is not asserted. `incidentEdges_mono` and `relevantCostEdges_subset_next` supply the monotonicity needed to compare distinct remaining circuit suffix lengths.

**Mathematical assumptions and exact method.** For naturals q,N, χ:DirichletCharacter ℂ q, sigma:Real with 1<sigma and N>=3, arbitrary real T, and real t for pointwise comparison: s=sigma+i*t and a_n=dirichletVonMangoldtCoefficient q χ n. Define K(sigma,T)=(2*pi)^(-1)*integral_{-T}^T (1/(sigma+i*t)) dt, with inherited oriented real-parameter integral. Then exact term integral is a_N*K(sigma,T) and the endpoint error norm is norm(a_N)*norm(K(sigma,T)-1/2). This yields the genuinely quantitative but nonasymptotic bound norm(finitePerronHeightDefect) <= sum_{n<N} norm(finitePerronTermIntegral(n)-a_n) + norm(a_N)*norm(K(sigma,T)-1/2). In particular K(sigma,0)=0, disproving any unconditional assertion that a finite-height endpoint integral always equals a_N/2. No assumption or proof of K(sigma,T) converging to 1/2 is smuggled into these theorems. No hSeven or global prime error is used.

## Johnston–Yang: a uniform negative prime–cofactor Gram family

**Source.** Daniel R. Johnston and Andrew Yang, *Some explicit estimates for the error term in the prime number theorem*, arXiv:2204.01980v2, 20 April 2022 (document date 21 April), https://arxiv.org/pdf/2204.01980v2; published DOI https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jmaa.2023.127460. Exact imported written result: Corollary 1.3, equation (1.6), page 2, for every real x>=2, |pi(x)-li(x)|<=9.59 x(log x)^0.515 exp(-0.8274 sqrt(log x)); li is the principal-value logarithmic integral from 0. Primary pages 1–2 inspected; no historical influence inferred.

## OpenAI adapter reconciliation: ordinary correlations, seven-eighths, and weighted budgets

Local attribution: grwtsk contributors. This entry reconciles the provider references
and the two previously unindexed signed-Abel supplements with the canonical ledger.
It does not replace historical source identities or promote their verification status.

### Paper sources and exact imported hypotheses

- **Ordinary correlations:** OpenAI, *Ordinary two-point correlations of multiplicative functions*, September 24, 2026. [Pinned paper](https://github.com/openai/math/blob/7ef5c0cc5ca4b0e072b7a050fca09ab24bc6fa3a/preprints/Ordinary-two-point-correlations-of-multiplicative-functions-September-24-2026/final.pdf), PDF blob `a1fa025a59ecbfc5176d16cfdc6e0afd418cc82e`, README blob `965a26b87458fb541e5a0a36d40804ab86cadad7`. Upstream commit `7ef5c0cc5ca4b0e072b7a050fca09ab24bc6fa3a`; Apache-2.0 and the genuine OpenAI byline are preserved. No DOI/arXiv identifier is invented. `OAI.OrdinaryTwoPointCorrelations.liouville_log_saving` supplies one positive exponent and a positive constant for each **fixed** positive-slope affine pair with nonzero determinant, for real cutoffs `X >= 3`. Constants retain form dependence. `binary_corrected_elliott` requires multiplicativity and one-boundedness of both complex functions, uniform nonpretentiousness of at least one, and distinct fixed shifts. `OAI.TwoPointCorrelations.UniformlyNonpretentious.finite_prime_change` preserves nonpretentiousness under bounded changes on one fixed finite prime set; multiplicativity of the modified function remains a separate premise. Sources: `lean/OAI/NumberTheory/TwoPointCorrelations/FinalMain.lean`, blob `2bd48811e73bbdc80c1f738fa889a14189186408`, and `lean/OAI/NumberTheory/TwoPoint/PretentiousDistance.lean`, blob `31e9010dee390175fcaca4f6e1b58c527d58ad2d`.
- **Seven-eighths gap:** OpenAI, *The Quasi-Riemann Hypothesis: A Zero-Free Half-Plane Re(s)>7/8*, September 30, 2026. [Pinned paper](https://github.com/openai/math/blob/7ef5c0cc5ca4b0e072b7a050fca09ab24bc6fa3a/preprints/The-Quasi-Riemann-Hypothesis-September-30-2026/paper.pdf), PDF blob `8132b77eeb270a70b25df8d6609f008e83e65d91`, README blob `05daa0838d60781c338ac22a9b06e6990f970981`. Same upstream commit; source `lean/OAI/NumberTheory/DirichletL/Nonvanishing.lean`, blob `46c67f4000906b1391a471d6e6396ac0da3c16a5`. `OAI.riemannZeta_ne_zero_of_seven_eighths_lt_re` and `OAI.DirichletCharacter.LFunction_ne_zero_of_seven_eighths_lt_re` exclude zeros for real part greater than `7/8`; the Dirichlet statement requires a nonzero modulus and exclusion of the principal pole. The real-gap consequence retains `q >= 3`, a nonprincipal character and an actual real L-function zero. No independent acceptance of these analytic inputs is asserted.

### New actual weighted-residual packet bridge

**Source pin:** math `c60c5243f18ee2473a898f1afe37890abe37964d`, path `GRWTSK/NumberTheory/Moebius/MertensSignedAbelCorrelationEnvelope.lean`, blob `6892df6058c1da52105e065320bd830ad76391f0`.
Fully qualified new declarations are:

- `GRWTSK.NumberTheory.Moebius.MertensSignedAbelCorrelationEnvelope.bulkPrefixFunctional_le_two_mul_of_weighted_budget`
- `GRWTSK.NumberTheory.Moebius.MertensSignedAbelCorrelationEnvelope.abs_bulk_sub_model_le_two_mul_weighted_budget`
- `GRWTSK.NumberTheory.Moebius.MertensSignedAbelCorrelationEnvelope.packet_le_two_add_model_add_two_mul_weighted_budget`

**Exact hypotheses and use:** `j : Nat`, `width : Nat -> Nat`, real arrays on
natural shift/prefix pairs, `B >= 0`, and a bound by `B` for each retained depth's
sum of the **actual** `normalizedSignedAbelShiftKernel j t h * E h (t+1)` over
`1 <= h < 2^(j+1)`. The residual theorem separately assumes
`abs(F h (t+1)-G h (t+1)) <= E h (t+1)` at exactly those retained depths and
shifts. The first theorem proves `bulkPrefixFunctional width j E <= 2*B`, using
its literal `2/Y` factor, `Y=2^(j+1)`, and zero contribution from excluded depths.
The second bounds the model residual by `2*B`. The actual packet consumer takes
`F=mertensTwoPointPrefix`, `width=sqrtWidth`, and proves
`weightedMertensDyadicSignedAbelDepthPacket j <= 2 + bulkPrefixFunctional sqrtWidth j G + 2*B`.
The inherited boundary cost two is paid once and the model stays signed.

This improves use of the existing error envelope when its large entries have
small actual kernel weight; it does not require a pointwise uniform envelope.
It is an independently derived finite inequality and source-exact GRWTSK
consumer, not a new imported OpenAI estimate. The arithmetic residual supplier,
the weighted budget for the actual source, and the signed-model/all-positive-
exponent growth budget remain distinct missing bridges. No Liouville-to-Moebius
substitution, moving-family constant control, unconditional RH or historical
influence is inferred.

### Provider consumer continuation (2026-10-28)

Local attribution: grwtsk contributors. This continuation extends the exact pinned
OpenAI/Anthropic entries above; it changes no upstream dependency or source identity.

- `GRWTSK.NumberTheory.Moebius.MertensSignedAbelCorrelationEnvelope.riemannHypothesis_of_weighted_model_correlation_budget`
  is a **conditional source proof candidate** for the existing local RH consumer.
  For every dyadic index it requires the true Mertens two-point residual bound at
  source length t+1, and a bound B(j)≥0 on its normalized signed-Abel weighted
  envelope at every retained depth. It additionally requires, for every δ>0,
  one nonnegative C uniform in j bounding the signed model plus 2B(j) by
  C(2^j)^δ. It reduces these hypotheses to the existing envelope RH interface;
  it supplies neither the arithmetic model/residual nor the all-exponent growth
  premise. Fixed-shift qualitative OpenAI convergence supplies neither.
- `GRWTSK.NumberTheory.AnthropicMath.Fermat.powerSum_eq_forces_zero_factor`
  is a **source proof candidate** for natural-number Diophantine power-sum search:
  n≥3 and a^n+b^n=c^n imply a=0 or b=0, with no positivity premise on a,b,c.
  It explicitly rules out c=0 when both summands are positive, then consumes the
  pinned Anthropic FLT transport. This strengthens the consumer interface to
  include degenerate input/output cases; it is not an independent Wiles proof.
  The original paper identities, public authors, upstream notices and FLT pin
  recorded above remain the provenance boundary.

### Scope of the original mathematics and references

- PFR: the repository formalizes the Polynomial Freiman–Ruzsa/Marton result
  ([upstream paper reference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.05762)). Exact hypotheses
  and quantitative constants remain those of the imported declarations. No
  local entropy/group representation comparison is supplied by an import.
- PNT+ and IEANTN: prime-counting and explicit-analytic-number-theory source
  interfaces retain their actual error terms, ranges and premise/evidence roles.
  Every versioned IEANTN solution project is separate from the core claim graph.
  Numerical certificates and stated literature dependencies are not automatically
  kernel-checked analytic inputs.
- Carleson: the generalized Carleson source retains its metric/measure/operator
  parameters and exceptional-set hypotheses. Sphere packing retains its exact
  dimensions, density conventions and analytic source inputs. An import supplies
  no missing local normalization or operator-equivalence proof.
- Imperial FLT: imports include the actual assumptions and intermediate
  arithmetic interfaces, not an assertion that every blueprint target is proved.
  This independent source identity is not conflated with Anthropic's FLT package.
- APAP: quantitative progression results retain their finite ambient groups,
  density conditions, logarithmic conventions and constants. Equational theories
  retains equation identifiers, implication/countermodel scope and finiteness
  domains; its executable/tooling layers are not a semantic soundness theorem.
- Formal Conjectures: all declared libraries are available as explicitly marked
  statement interfaces. Deliberate conjecture/challenge `sorry` placeholders are
  not proved propositions. No local theorem consumes them as accepted proofs.
- plby/lean-proofs: all ten maintained Lake subprojects found at the pin are
  separate adapters, including the versioned ComparatorChallenges packages.
  Root README, source metadata and individual file bylines preserve the actual
  informal/formal authors; there is no invented common paper or blanket proof
  claim. Its latest package has effectful post-update patch hooks. They remain
  in the upstream dependency configuration and were **NOT_EXECUTED**; source
  replay must bind any actually patched bytes separately from original Git pins.
- Lean Liquid: native Lean 3.48.0, upstream Mathlib3 commit
  `5947fb69cc1fdfebaba1e1b1f0a04f26f0f612bf`, imports `liquid`. This is historical
  imported mathematics, not a Lean4 port or cross-kernel equivalence proof.
  The original project references Peter Scholze's *Analytic* lecture notes
  (<http://www.math.uni-bonn.de/people/scholze/Analytic.pdf>); no new formalization
  of those notes is claimed. No repository-wide license was invented where the
  source tree has no root license file; original source notices stay upstream.

Exact public bylines and license obligations remain in the immutable upstream
sources; available repository LICENSE/NOTICE files are retained per provider.
Source provenance is independent of broad MSC2020 folder placement. The source
layout record explicitly marks these new broad library assignments and does not
claim a declaration-by-declaration semantic taxonomy review.

### pfr

On a conjecture of Marton; W.T.Gowers, Ben Green, Freddie Manners, Terence Tao; 2023 arXiv:2311.05762. Source README attributes exponent-nine refinement to Jyun-Jie Liao. This consumer uses exact pinned refined source rather than claiming the original paper stated exponent9. Apache-2.0; upstream original authors/bylines retained through dependency.

G arbitrary AddCommGroup and Module(ZMod2); finite nonempty A; Nat.card(A+A)<=K*Nat.card(A). No ambient finiteness assumption. Local Finset interface additionally needs DecidableEq G.

finite subgroup H size<=|A| and finite difference-center set D with |D|<(2*K^9)^2 and A-A subset D+H; natural powers and strict source constant preserved.

Use: Existing local APIs use Finsets and difference sets. Proves subtraction transport and exact Finset/set cardinal and pointwise bridges rather than leaving them as assumed interfaces. Does not yet combine with local energy inequalities.

### apap

### equational-theories

Equational theories project proof calculus and completeness/compactness source, pinned 2026 repository revision. Original upstream author and license notices remain in source; no historical paper influence inferred. Apache-2.0, exact upstream notices retained.

A context Gamma of magma laws on Type u; DecidableEq of variables for extracted Finset support. Semantic consequence uses the upstream all-model domain; countermodels are arbitrary Type with a Magma instance, not finite-only.

A proof uses finitely many axioms all belonging to Gamma; semantic consequence has a finite Delta subset Gamma. Any slice retaining the full actual support suffices. Countermodels block derivations; reflexivity has empty support, transitivity combines both supports.

Use: Concrete source-axiom extraction for the local joint-premise inference programme; strengthens imported compactness by retaining Delta subset Gamma, and proves safe chosen-proof support restriction.

### pnt-plus

Prime Number Theorem and More, Alex Kontorovich and Terence Tao (CITATION.cff version0.1.0; released2024-01-09, ongoing). Apache-2.0.

epsilon real >0. Eventually x real there exist natural p<q with both prime and x<p<q<(1+epsilon)x; consequently exists natural N=p+q with 2x<N<2(1+epsilon)x. Threshold depends on epsilon.

Compose two eventual relative-prime windows using delta=min(epsilon/4,1), then construct a target N=p+q from two distinct primes. Supplies actual prime-pair/sum witnesses, not arbitrary prescribed Goldbach targets.

### carleson

A blueprint for the formalization of Carleson’s theorem on convergence of Fourier series; Lars Becker, María Inés de Frutos-Fernández, Leo Diedering, Floris van Doorn, Sébastien Gouëzel, Asgar Jamneshan, Evgenia Karunus, Edward van de Meent, Pietro Monticone, Jasper Mulder-Sohn, Jim Portegies, Joris Roos, Michael Rothgang, Rajula Srivastava, James Sundstrom, Jeremy Tan, Christoph Thiele (pinned formalization.yaml). Apache-2.0.

Index type iota is Countable; f:iota->Real->Complex; each f_i Continuous and Function.Periodic(2*Real.pi); s:Finset iota, weights w:iota->Complex fixed independently of Fourier cutoff. Conclusion uses Lebesgue a.e. on Real, root partialFourierSum, atTop over natural truncations.

Countable family of continuous 2pi-periodic complex functions shares one full-measure convergence set; every fixed finite complex coefficient row has a convergent scalar Fourier readout and actual residual norm tending to zero on that set.

Use: Countable family of continuous 2pi-periodic complex functions shares one full-measure convergence set; every fixed finite complex coefficient row has a convergent scalar Fourier readout and actual residual norm tending to zero on that set.

## IEANTN: from a numerical theta error bound to an open square interval

Local attribution: grwtsk contributors. Imported proof source: Terence Tao and
IEANTN contributors, *Explicit Analytic Number Theory*, versioned repository
source at [teorth/IEANTN@ec66e388e6e117c6881367f38665f310edf7cacf](https://github.com/teorth/IEANTN/tree/ec66e388e6e117c6881367f38665f310edf7cacf); Apache-2.0.

`PrimeInterval.v1.Dev.numericalBound_hasPrimeInInterval`, source
`Solutions/PrimeInterval.v1/Solution.lean`, blob
`e1a89e5f31e5263353b0bc63a68ff99e020b7710`, is the consumed proved endpoint.

For `n : Nat`, `n >= 1`, retain the actual uniform premise
`IEANTN.HasNumericalBound IEANTN.Eθ ε x₀`, the applicability condition
`x₀ <= (n : Real)^2`, and the strict margin
`(2*(n : Real)^2 + (2*(n : Real)+1))*ε < 2*(n : Real)+1`.
The source criterion at `x=n²`, `h=2n+1` produces a prime in `(n²,(n+1)²]`.
The upper square is nonprime, so the witness lies in the open natural interval;
its `Nat.Prime` filter has positive cardinality. A separate interval equivalence
retains an arbitrary nonprime upper endpoint and includes empty/reversed intervals.

<a id="carleson-fixed-support-varying-rows-20261029"></a>

## Carleson: a fixed-support envelope for varying coefficient rows

Imported proof source remains `classical_carleson`,
`Carleson/Classical/ClassicalCarleson.lean`, blob
`cef99075c938b9a5351ef800b72e612345e4e071`, in
[fpvandoorn/carleson@52cba54d4b7b444e937c7abbc67835afa2a0bb16](https://github.com/fpvandoorn/carleson/blob/52cba54d4b7b444e937c7abbc67835afa2a0bb16/Carleson/Classical/ClassicalCarleson.lean).
The pinned project title/byline is *A blueprint for the formalization of
Carleson’s theorem on convergence of Fourier series*: Lars Becker,
María Inés de Frutos-Fernández, Leo Diedering, Floris van Doorn,
Sébastien Gouëzel, Asgar Jamneshan, Evgenia Karunus, Edward van de Meent,
Pietro Monticone, Jasper Mulder-Sohn, Jim Portegies, Joris Roos,
Michael Rothgang, Rajula Srivastava, James Sundstrom, Jeremy Tan and
Christoph Thiele; [project blueprint](https://florisvandoorn.com/carleson/blueprint/),
version bound by that Git pin. Apache-2.0; original public bylines remain intact.
No new paper-to-theorem mapping or broader Carleson-Hunt result is inferred.

Fix Countable iota, a finite support s (empty allowed), and a fixed family of
continuous 2*pi-periodic complex functions f_i. The actual scalar residual of
any row w with sum_s norm(w_i)<=1 is bounded pointwise by
sum_s norm(partialFourierSum_N(f_i)(x)-f_i(x)). This envelope tends to zero on
one common Lebesgue-full-measure set. Thus for each x there and each epsilon>0,
an eventual cutoff works simultaneously for every such row. It may depend on
x, epsilon, f and s, but not on w. An arbitrary sequence of admissible rows W(N)
therefore has residual norm tending to zero; W need not converge. The SAME W(N)
weights the Fourier approximation and moving reference sum_s W(N,i)*f_i(x).
This does not establish convergence of either moving readout itself.

The local source is `adapters/carleson/GRWTSK/FourierAnalysis/FourierAnalysisInOneVariable/FiniteFourierReadoutConvergence.lean`, commit `fd6f3d97e80088c09262adbf5fa7ccce8467d504`, blob
`a803cf300e8a4987ddfc62ff0bc1985617fd4734`. New declarations under
`GRWTSK.FourierAnalysis.FourierAnalysisInOneVariable.FiniteFourierReadoutConvergence`:

- `unit_ball_residual_le_coordinate_sum`
- `finite_unit_ball_residual_ae_uniform`
- `cutoff_dependent_unit_ball_residual_ae_tendsto_zero`

Actual local use: the finite scalar-observation residual shape in
`GRWTSK/RealFunctions/DiscreteFourierReadout.lean`, blob
`3f33ba2da169c76f021c899760245cc78db17f17`, at source parent
`08f98951548df4b4cd74b1db97801a80c38b6e44`. The continuous-periodic theorem supplies an actual vanishing
residual envelope, while a proved cyclic-DFT/circle-Fourier comparison remains
UNSUPPLIED. No growing support, rate, uniformity in x or in all continuous
functions, infinite-sum exchange or practical performance assertion follows.
The pointwise algebraic estimate needs no continuity or periodicity premise;
the source's Countable section-instance scope is retained in its proposed type
consumer, pending actual native elaboration.

Classification: three new SOURCE_PROOF_CANDIDATE consumer declarations.
The original three theorem bodies are unchanged; their historical source blob

and old verification targets keep their own scope. The new static target is

<a id="pfr-dense-submodule-slice-20261029"></a>

## PFR: a constructed dense submodule slice with its own additive energy

W. T. Gowers, Ben Green, Freddie Manners and Terence Tao,
[*On a conjecture of Marton*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.05762v2), 2023,
version2 dated 2023-12-12, is the original exponent-twelve PFR source.
Jyun-Jie Liao, [*Improved Exponent for Marton's Conjecture in F_2^n*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.09639v1),
2024-04-15 version1, states the exponent-nine refinement with at most 2*K^9
cosets and subgroup cardinality at most |A|. The pinned upstream
formalization.yaml blob ddee8cd72f0937e2a9ca349ff3f616b9d31c8be3 maps its
refined main result to Liao's paper but does not specify an arXiv version;
the version identities above were independently rechecked, not retroactively
attributed to that metadata.

Consumed native endpoint: `better_PFR_conjecture` in
`teorth/pfr@7e7fadf0c4d000fdb21b68505f3fd0e29a6e4a2f`,
PFR/RhoFunctional.lean blob af3f89faaf4f12edd2ec9796b93ae77474a99c70.
Its exact type accepts a finite nonempty set in an arbitrary ZMod2 module;
no finiteness of the ambient group is assumed by this source interface. The
paper's finite-vector-space statement and the native type retain their distinct
source identities. Public upstream bylines and Apache-2.0 obligations remain intact.

For nonempty Finset A with |A+A|<=K|A|, the local construction selects a largest
slice from the actual finite coset-center cover and translates it into the SAME
finite submodule H. Overlapping/repeated cosets are permitted by the union bound.
It constructs nonempty B exactly equal to {b in H | u+b in A}, with
Nat |H|<=|A|, Real |A|<2*K^(9:Nat)*|B|, and B-B subset H.
The existing Mathlib additive-energy Cauchy--Schwarz theorem then gives
Nat |B|^2*|B|^2 <= |H|*Finset.addEnergy B B. Both support and energy use that
same B and H; no density estimate is left as a free supplied field.
The general energy helper also covers empty B.

Local source `adapters/pfr/GRWTSK/Combinatorics/DenseSubmoduleSlice.lean`, commit `9a913f5c7e184d75e8701096c5c5a59b144c2793`, blob
`83d53ed5afb4254d155a461eeb3bc989498ed554`. Fully qualified declarations:

- `GRWTSK.Combinatorics.DenseSubmoduleSlice.translated_slice_of_finite_cover`
- `GRWTSK.Combinatorics.DenseSubmoduleSlice.card_pow_four_le_submodule_card_mul_energy`
- `GRWTSK.Combinatorics.DenseSubmoduleSlice.dense_submodule_slice_of_small_doubling`

Actual local uses are the finite-set small-doubling and energy families:
GRWTSK/Combinatorics/VerySmallDoubling.lean blob8618c51d439b44b5fe633a9c0bb8e0b2a503be13
and GRWTSK/Combinatorics/AdditiveEnergy.lean blobbc60e7dd0bc91351780ecc1a0e2b7176fda530ed,
at e34255f988c9f729858c94d28be36f264021f168. The pinned Mathlib Energy API is
consumed directly, without a root import or duplicate energy definition.
This is an alternative structural route to the earlier difference-cover
consumer, not a claim that its existential H is the same witness as a different
call, nor a supplied <3/2 doubling hypothesis or energy bound for A itself.

Sphere Packing in Lean project authors/version entry. The optimal-density
MainTheorem placeholder remains excluded. This is an elementary local metric
covering consequence, not a new paper-to-theorem mapping.

For a finite sample S of E8 points and NNReal epsilon with strict 2*epsilon<sqrt2,
the external covering number of its exact WithLp2 Euclidean image equals S.card
(as ENat). Thus every closed-ball cover C has at least S.card centers; C may be
infinite and its centers need not lie in E8 or S. Empty S and epsilon=0 are
included. Strictness is retained because the cover uses closed balls. There is
no claim at the half-separation boundary, no lattice-density/volume asymptotic,
and no algorithmic cost assertion.

<a id="anthropic-primitive-zero-readouts-20261029"></a>

## Primitive-character local zero counts as actual finite response envelopes

[Levent Alpöge and Ralph Furman, *More than two thirds of the zeta zeros are simple
and on the critical line*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13637v2), version 2 dated
2026-08-19 (first submitted 2026-08-13), is the package's companion paper.
The consumed endpoint here is specifically the source-internal local Jensen-count
method `Zeta23.ThmE.localCountChi_uniform_proof`, not a headline proportion theorem.
It lives in zeta23/Zeta23/ThmE/LocalCountChi.lean, blob
603d34e2374063f482905e72c84e5666f23275ad, at anthropics/formal-math
16a5ce41304fcdaea4e0be7c132e1c361dc26da5. The NcountL definition layer is
Zeta23/ThmE/Statement.lean blob1aaec6accad36bad8fb204a4b974a58524d3bc98.
Upstream metadata credits Claude; Anthropic 2026 copyright, Apache-2.0 and modified
PNT+ vendor/Mathlib notices remain preserved. No new historical influence or
paper-to-support-lemma equivalence is inferred from the title.

The source supplies ONE A>=1 before every q [NeZero q], q>1, every index Typeu,
finite family S and selected primitive characters chi_i, all at the common q,
and every real t/complex coefficient row K. The derived readout bound is
norm(sum_S K_i*NcountL(chi_i,t,t+1)) <= A*log(q*(abs(t)+3))*sum_S norm(K_i).
The logarithm is proved positive (its argument is at least 6), and the SAME A
bounds the normalized readout on the l1 unit ball. Repeated character labels
and empty S are allowed; no primality of q or fixed-family constant is introduced.
This is an actual bound, not a supplied free envelope and not cancellation/decay.

NcountL counts zeros of the L-function in 0<Re(rho)<1, t<Im(rho)<=t+1, WITH
multiplicity via analyticOrderAt.toNat. Every real t is retained. Negative
ordinates and real nontrivial zeros in windows meeting 0 are not discarded;
the folder heading imposes no nonreality hypothesis. Distinct/simple/critical-line
counts are not substituted. Neither RH, a zero-proportion improvement, Mertens
cancellation nor a growing-family Liouville estimate follows.

Local source `adapters/anthropic-math/zeta23/GRWTSK/NumberTheory/ZetaAndLFunctionsAnalyticTheory/NonrealZerosOfZetaSAndLSChiRiemannAndOtherHypotheses/PrimitiveCharacterZeroCountReadouts.lean`, commit `63ea24924f18f87132e153ef59fb02c0e9aa314b`, blob `98f0405c0218b9839a46bf664df20d9d1b31e36a`.
