<a id="apap-affine-support-20261029"></a>

### APAP: an actual affine support of three distinct points

Local attribution: grwtsk contributors. Classification: SOURCE_PROOF_CANDIDATE.
The upstream APAP/Mathlib sources retain their public bylines and Apache-2.0 notices.

The existing Roth consumer supplies failure of `ThreeAPFree`, while the pinned
APAP witness gives `a+c=b+b` and `a≠b`. A finite configuration needs three distinct
points. The new construction proves the missing separation using invertibility
of scalar2 in `ZMod q`, sets the actual step `r=b-a`, and returns an embedding
`e : Fin 3 ↪ G` with `e i = a+i.val • r`. The finite support `univ.map e` lies in
the SAME `A` and has cardinality3. No new pairwise-distinctness premise is assumed.

Generic transport: `G : Type u`, `AddCommGroup G`, `Module (ZMod q) G`, prime
`q≥3`, `a+c=b+b` and `a≠b`; no finiteness premise. Density application additionally
retains `Fintype G`, nonempty `A : Finset G`, real `δ>0`, `δ≤A.dens`, and STRICT
`2^148*(1+log(δ⁻¹))^9 < Module.finrank (ZMod q) G`, with natural powers over Real.
The exact scalar in the affine map is the natural number `i.val`, not a new Fin/ZMod encoding.

Paper context: Thomas F. Bloom and Olof Sisask, *The Kelley–Meka bounds for sets
free of three-term arithmetic progressions*, [arXiv:2302.07211v3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2302.07211v3),
2025-05-13 (first version2023). Its finite-field Theorem2 assumes an odd prime
and a three-progression-free subset of a finite vector space; the source `ff`
supplies its own explicit rank normalization. The literal `2^148` is not
attributed to an unspecified paper constant. The upstream README links this
paper; the separate improvement paper2309.02353 is not substituted for it.

<a id="anthropic-fermat-integer-fibers-20261029"></a>

### Integer Fermat fibers: parity and the signed zero-output boundary

Local attribution: grwtsk contributors. SOURCE_PROOF_CANDIDATE, not kernel acceptance.

An integer Diophantine solver cannot reuse the natural two-axis fiber blindly.
For `n≥3`, the actual proved source endpoint first excludes triples with ALL
three coordinates nonzero. Mathlib's proved `fermatLastTheoremFor_iff_int`
transports the source `flt_mathlib` to integers. There is no free FLT premise.
Thus equality gives `a=0 ∨ b=0 ∨ c=0`; reducing this to only the first two
alternatives would discard legitimate odd zero-output solutions.

For natural exponent `n≥3` and integers `a,b,c`, the odd classification takes
`Odd n` and is exactly
`a^n+b^n=c^n ↔ (a=0∧b=c) ∨ (b=0∧a=c) ∨ (c=0∧b=-a)`.
The fixed-output set is `{(0,c),(c,0)} ∪ {p : ℤ×ℤ | c=0 ∧ p.2=-p.1}`.
When `c=0` the entire antidiagonal remains, including `(1,-1)`; for nonzero
output only the two axis points remain. No positivity restrictions are added.

For `Even n`, equality is exactly
`(a=0∧(b=c∨b=-c)) ∨ (b=0∧(a=c∨a=-c))`; the fixed-output set is
`{(0,c),(0,-c),(c,0),(-c,0)}`. These are four candidates as a SET, not four
distinct solutions: they all coincide at output zero. Even powers are
nonnegative, so the zero-output case forces both inputs to vanish.
Odd power injectivity and even equality-up-to-sign identify actual coordinates,
not only their powers/absolute values. Exponent2 is excluded throughout.

Paper/source context retains the existing [Wiles/Taylor-Wiles ledger entry](#anthropic-adapter-reconciliation):
Andrew Wiles, *Modular elliptic curves and Fermat’s Last Theorem* (1995),
[DOI10.2307/2118559](https://doi.org/10.2307/2118559), and Richard Taylor and
Andrew Wiles, *Ring-theoretic properties of certain Hecke algebras* (1995),
[DOI10.2307/2118560](https://doi.org/10.2307/2118560). The upstream project
describes a strategy adaptation, not a statement-by-statement paper translation.
The new parity classification is an elementary local consequence of the
imported FLT endpoint and pinned transport, not an independent Wiles proof.
Anthropic PBC, Imperial FLT, flt-regular and Mathlib public notices remain intact
in UPSTREAM-ATTRIBUTION/UPSTREAM-LICENSE/UPSTREAM-NOTICE and dependencies.

<a id="formal-conjectures-erdos-moser-positive-20261029"></a>

### Formal Conjectures: positive Erdős–Moser sums and the open exclusion

Local attribution: grwtsk contributors. Three public SOURCE_PROOF_CANDIDATES;
the private Gauss helper belongs to complete compiler-owned discovery/auditing.
The Formal Conjectures Authors (2026) and Mathlib retain their Apache-2.0 notices.

The exact imported definition is `ErdosMoser.powerSum k m = ∑ i∈Ico 1 m, i^k`.
The first local result proves `powerSum k(n+1)=∑ i∈range n,(i+1)^k` for ALL
natural `k,n`, including exponent zero and empty range. The shift retains positive
bases; inserting a zero base would change the exponent-zero observation.

A division-free Gauss identity gives `powerSum 1(n+1)*2=(n+1)*n`.
The second public result cancels genuinely positive factors and proves, for
STRICT `0<m`, `powerSum 1 m=m^1 ↔ m=3`. Input `m=1` is included and handled;
`m=0` must be excluded since positive-exponent equations hold there. This is an
all-positive-input classification, not promotion of the upstream `(1,3)` test.

The third result proves equivalence between the raw positive statement
`∀k m,0<k→0<m→powerSum k m=m^k→k=1∧m=3`
and `∀k m,2≤k→0<m→powerSum k m≠m^k`.
**The right-hand universal exclusion is UNSUPPLIED. This is a statement reduction,
not a proof of the Erdős–Moser conjecture.** No authored proof uses the admitted
`ErdosMoser.erdos_moser_conjecture` or imported finite test endpoints.

Source/problem identity: `google-deepmind/formal-conjectures@909a8bec155bc5a6f3c72e12baaaa610ae293281`,
`FormalConjectures/Wikipedia/ErdosMoser.lean` blob `52cd5fc024d23ecdd04a4b7c43a4f679c6549f07`,
exact consumed declaration `ErdosMoser.powerSum`; LICENSE blob `d645695673349e3947e8e5ae42332d0ac3164cd7`.
The module cites Bernd C. Kellner, *On stronger conjectures that imply the
Erdös-Moser conjecture*, [arXiv:1003.1646v2](https://arxiv.org/abs/1003.1646v2),
2012-09-05 (first submitted2010-03-08), J.Number Theory131(2011),1054–1061,
[DOI10.1016/j.jnt.2011.01.004](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jnt.2011.01.004).
That is BACKGROUND_ONLY: no particular stronger-conjecture implication from
Kellner is consumed. The new index/Gauss argument is elementary local work.

<a id="e8-cover-matching-20261029"></a>
### E8 radius-close covering matchings (2026-10-29 source candidates)

Local attribution: grwtsk contributors. Source basis: *Sphere Packing in Lean*, ongoing version0.1.0 (released2025-06-13), Christopher Birkbeck, Sidharth Hariharan, Seewoo Lee, Ho Kiu Gareth Ma, Bhavik Mehta and Maryna Viazovska. The consumed [Basic.E8 source](https://github.com/thefundamentaltheor3m/Sphere-Packing-Lean/blob/77cf0a0c104f2e31501eab04d88bbc068d61c169/SpherePacking/Basic/E8.lean) has copyright2025 Bhavik Mehta and authors Bhavik Mehta/Gareth Ma, Apache-2.0; commit `77cf0a0c104f2e31501eab04d88bbc068d61c169`, blob `c369209e9ae2a7e358e2b312cd251bf9caf20cd2`. The exact endpoint `E8_norm_lower_bound` says an E8 vector is zero or has Euclidean `WithLp.toLp 2` norm at least `sqrt 2`. The admitted `SpherePacking.MainTheorem` is excluded. No new external paper theorem mapping or attribution of this matching result to an optimal-density paper is asserted.

The method chooses an actual covering center for each point and uses the E8 separation plus triangle inequality to prove injectivity. The finite equal-cardinality case uses `Fintype.bijective_iff_injective_and_card` and `Equiv.ofBijective` with the same forward map. <a id="plby-subset-collision-20261030"></a>
### Reciprocal mass produces disjoint positive subset-sum witnesses (2026-10-30 source candidates)

Local attribution: grwtsk contributors. Paper statement: S. J. Benkoski and P. Erdős, *On Weird and Pseudoperfect Numbers*, Mathematics of Computation28(126),617–623 (1974), [DOI10.2307/2005938](https://doi.org/10.2307/2005938), [authors' archive](https://users.renyi.hu/~p_erdos/1974-24.pdf). Theorem1 gives `sum_i1/ai<2` for positive distinct natural generators whose subset sums are all distinct; the paper credits C. Ryavec's proof. The consumed Lean endpoint is this finite strict bound, not a stronger refinement, equality classification, infinite extension or weird-number theorem. The selected formal source uses finite subset-sum counting/prefix comparison and reciprocal-sum comparison. Only the theorem endpoint is mapped to Theorem1; no historical proof-route equivalence is supplied.

[Imported Erdos350 source](https://github.com/plby/lean-proofs/blob/8822f7ddef30fadbd92e1c6ab4ed897af356af5e/src/v4.29.1/ErdosProblems/Erdos350.lean): plby/lean-proofs `8822f7ddef30fadbd92e1c6ab4ed897af356af5e`, `src/v4.29.1/ErdosProblems/Erdos350.lean` / blob `4bd7afe5e89ab92209eee9453031c5e80f6fe314`, SHA256 `e2f0129a7dfb5b1ea5593859242eb9c42e23da08bb5f4588c89e09aeca5e8d41`. Exact endpoint `Erdos350.erdos_350` assumes `Erdos350.DecidableDistinctSubsetSums A` for `A:Finset Nat` and concludes the REAL reciprocal sum is strictly below2; exclusion of zero follows internally from the empty/singleton subset comparison. Source informal bylines Charles Albert Ryavec and ChatGPT, formal bylines Aristotle and Boris Alexeev are preserved. Version notice blob `28334966307b373f23c1e929e128c84c5980066e` applies Apache2 to some external-source files only; the selected leaf has no explicit grant, so its license grant remains **UNRESOLVED**, not collection-wide Apache. The source is referenced by immutable import, not copied into math; Mathlib retains its separate Apache2 license.

[New local source](https://github.com/grwtsk/math/blob/d38bc7c2e8b8cc45dad12180ff5bdbe5808deb66/adapters/lean-proofs/v4.29.1/GRWTSK/NumberTheory/AdditiveNumberTheory/ReciprocalSubsetSumCollision.lean) at math `d38bc7c2e8b8cc45dad12180ff5bdbe5808deb66` / blob `fb19960a95c6405f8cff395cbff9791111f8a37c`, SHA256 `58bfcf82bb2a95a076e5522e9d18bc2a417610337d6b1081d053a950d46bb4e0`, contains:

- `GRWTSK.NumberTheory.AdditiveNumberTheory.ReciprocalSubsetSumCollision.disjoint_positive_supports_of_collision`
- `GRWTSK.NumberTheory.AdditiveNumberTheory.ReciprocalSubsetSumCollision.disjoint_positive_supports_of_reciprocal_mass`

The helper takes positive ambient `A:Finset Nat`, distinct `X,Y⊆A` with equal NAT sums, and constructs actual `S=X\Y,T=Y\X`, both nonempty/disjoint/subsets of A with equal strictly positive NAT sums. The main theorem takes an arbitrary `A:Finset Nat` and ONLY `2≤sum_(a∈A)(1/(a:Real))`. It returns two disjoint nonempty supports BOTH contained in the SAME `A.erase0`, with equal strictly positive natural sums. No input positivity, nonemptiness, cardinality, maximum bound or universe hypothesis is added. Removing zero preserves the exact REAL reciprocal mass (`1/0=0`); the source contraposition supplies an actual collision there, and common-support cancellation plus positivity supplies the returned supports.

Actual consumer: `GRWTSK.NumberTheory.AdditiveNumberTheory.DistinctSubsetSums.SumDistinct` and its counting lemmas in `GRWTSK/NumberTheory/DistinctSubsetSums.lean` at math `3e30691fe4e2035e2f799a4b146cec30dbafd880` / blob `4128530ca26874505217aeab1758b383137d68ea` observe injectivity of the powerset-sum map. The root model is neither duplicated nor imported across versions. Native Lean4.29.1/Mathlib `5e932f97dd25535344f80f9dd8da3aab83df0fe6` remains isolated from root4.34.1; root representation/version integration remains **UNSUPPLIED**.

For orientation, A={1,2,3,6} has reciprocal mass2 and supports {1,2}/{3} with sum3. A={1,2,3} already has such a collision below mass2, so the threshold is sufficient, not necessary. A={0,1} shows zero erasure does not preserve full subset-sum injectivity; only the stated reciprocal observation is preserved. Cancellation need not preserve the original common sum, and no minimal-support relation or effective search/runtime result is supplied. The stronger absolute-constant exponential lower bound of Erdős Problem1 remains open here.

Local attribution: grwtsk contributors. The selected declaration `FreyCurve.j_valuation_of_bad_prime` proves `(P.p:Int) ∣ padicValRat q P.freyCurve.j`; its introductory overview's “multiple of q” is a prose typo, not the type used here.

[New source](https://github.com/grwtsk/math/blob/b13149227de39dc96dc9074796508957949128bd/adapters/imperial-flt/GRWTSK/NumberTheory/ArithmeticAlgebraicGeometryDiophantineGeometry/FreyPrimePowerDenominator.lean), math `b13149227de39dc96dc9074796508957949128bd` / blob `2fa84acb0d2002740ca8bbd3da8565cf1a0edbd0`, SHA256 `4ec58adbba5d4f606834104784871a0a515da159194444f2b9f6a1776e990bfd`:

- `GRWTSK.NumberTheory.ArithmeticAlgebraicGeometryDiophantineGeometry.FreyPrimePowerDenominator.exponent_dvd_denominator_order`
- `GRWTSK.NumberTheory.ArithmeticAlgebraicGeometryDiophantineGeometry.FreyPrimePowerDenominator.prime_power_factor_of_denominator_prime`

ALL package hypotheses remain bundled in `P:FreyPackage`: signed integers a,b,c, each nonzero; p:Nat prime and p≥5; a^p+b^p=c^p; gcd(a,b)=1; a=3 in ZMod4 and b=0 in ZMod2. No positivity or actual package/Fermat counterexample is supplied. Both roots separately take q:Nat prime, 2<q and `(q:Int)∣P.a*P.b*P.c`. The first proves `P.p∣padicValNat q P.freyCurve.j.den`, including order0. The second additionally requires the actual NAT observation `q∣P.freyCurve.j.den`; it constructs a positive k with the SAME reduced denominator `den(j)=q^P.p*k`, gives `q^P.p≤den(j)` and `P.p≤padicValNat q den(j)`. That extra denominator premise is not inferred from the bad-prime condition.

Proof strategy: the canonical rational numerator and denominator have at least one zero q-adic order. In the numerator-zero branch, signed rational valuation is the negative denominator order; integer sign/cast divisibility transfers the source p-divisibility to Nat. In the denominator-zero branch divisibility of0 is retained. The extra denominator observation gives positive order; a positive multiple of p has order≥p, so the exact prime-power divisibility API supplies the positive factor witness. This is a conditional derived arithmetic consumer, not a constructor of its hypotheses.

Actual root use is the denominator/height family `GRWTSK.NumberTheory.Diophantine.PrimitiveAdditiveTriples.FreyMarkedJInvariant.j_denominator` and `.jHeight_source_comparison` in `GRWTSK/NumberTheory/DiophantineEquations/FreyMarkedJInvariant.lean` at `7b5963a8a72495c7a437498e993bd0d36579f77d` / blob `652ba3d843fc1dd64bfa4ec5b10524fef5391c82`. This increment constructs the native denominator factor; same-curve/invariant comparison and Lean4.35.0→root4.34.1 transport remain **UNSUPPLIED**. No root model/import/dependency is copied or upgraded.

<a id="carleson-convergent-coefficient-readouts-20261030"></a>
## Fixed-support Fourier readout limits with convergent coefficients — 2026-10-30

Local attribution: grwtsk contributors. Source/blueprint: *A blueprint for the formalization of Carleson’s theorem on convergence of Fourier series*, Lars Becker, María Inés de Frutos-Fernández, Leo Diedering, Floris van Doorn, Sébastien Gouëzel, Asgar Jamneshan, Evgenia Karunus, Edward van de Meent, Pietro Monticone, Jasper Mulder-Sohn, Jim Portegies, Joris Roos, Michael Rothgang, Rajula Srivastava, James Sundstrom, Jeremy Tan, Christoph Thiele. Version: immutable Carleson `52cba54d4b7b444e937c7abbc67835afa2a0bb16`; the pinned LaTeX header uses `\today`, so no immutable publication year is inferred. [Blueprint](https://florisvandoorn.com/carleson/blueprint/); pinned title blob `986ebbf96b6a42a1941ef60c96b2110b18ff10f3`, project blueprint byline `9e3f0dc048f47aa5a5fb63f05c04c2e02a0ba426`. This is source provenance for the existing continuous classical theorem, not a new numbered-paper theorem attribution or a claim that every project author wrote the selected headerless Lean file. Public upstream authorship and Apache-2.0 notices remain in the dependency.

Actual consumer: the existing isolated `FiniteFourierReadoutConvergence` module (blob `a803cf300e8a4987ddfc62ff0bc1985617fd4734`) controls residuals against the SAME moving coefficient row, which alone need not give a readout limit. New source `adapters/carleson/GRWTSK/FourierAnalysis/FourierAnalysisInOneVariable/ConvergentFourierReadouts.lean` at math `4edb8f1f095048b162361a5e4da25f8fcea2cca8`, blob `60d962300a1ef348e196546bc527598abd0605c9` / SHA256 `81d595d9a01926cf7131d6b89f9959e0d2463918d3732f68b5fbc26f53191cd3`, gives two roots in `GRWTSK.FourierAnalysis.FourierAnalysisInOneVariable.ConvergentFourierReadouts`: `convergent_coefficients_readout_ae` and `convergent_coefficients_fixed_residual_ae_tendsto_zero`.

For one fixed countable family of continuous `2*pi`-periodic functions `f_i:Real→Complex`, **one Lebesgue-full-measure event precedes every finite support s, coefficient sequence W and limiting row w**. On that event, the actual premise `W(N,i)→w(i)` for each `i∈s` gives `sum_(i∈s) W(N,i)*partialFourierSum N(f_i)(x) → sum_(i∈s) w(i)*f_i(x)`. Its residual norm from that FIXED limiting-weight reference tends to zero. Support stays fixed across natural cutoff N; empty support and countably infinite ambient index sets are allowed. No l1 bound or convergence outside s is assumed. The coefficient convergence is a premise, not a supplied family construction. The proof multiplies actual scalar/coordinate limits then takes a finite sum; it never intersects uncountably many separately selected full-measure sets. Cutoffs may depend on x,s,W,w and their convergence. No growing support, uniform cutoff/rate, infinite-sum exchange or one common event for all continuous families is supplied.

Imported endpoint: `classical_carleson`, source `Carleson/Classical/ClassicalCarleson.lean` blob `cef99075c938b9a5351ef800b72e612345e4e071`; the actual `partialFourierSum` definition is in `Carleson/Defs.lean` blob `4d3e1554ff28603cc2d5a808f1756ddc5f971d49`, with symmetric integer cutoff and AddCircle(2*pi) normalization. ## Union-closed small members and Collatz positive descent

Local attribution: grwtsk contributors. Source commit:
`9cea61d877c8c4089e9274454af886d665acc231`, reachable from `main`.
[Exact-source verification issue #1434](https://github.com/grwtsk/math/issues/1434).
These are elementary formalizations of known partial results and a conditional
reduction, with no claim of novelty or solution of either open problem.

### Union-closed families: singleton and doubleton members

**Source.** Henning Bruhn and Oliver Schaudt, *The journey of the union-closed
sets conjecture*, arXiv:1309.3297v2, 30 October 2013,
https://arxiv.org/abs/1309.3297v2, Sections 4–5. The survey attributes the small-member
observations to D. G. Sarvate and J.-C. Renaud. Supporting source mapping:
Douglas B. West, *Union-Closed Sets Conjecture (1979)*,
https://www.dwest.web.illinois.edu/openp/unionclos.html, partial-results discussion.
Historical priority beyond these explicit source attributions is not inferred.

**Exact object and hypotheses.** `F : Finset (Finset α)` for an arbitrary type
with decidable equality. Distinctness of family members is enforced by `Finset`.
`UnionClosed F` means every union of two members belongs to `F`; the empty set
need not be a member. `frequency F x` counts members containing `x`;
`Abundant F x` means `F.card ≤ 2 * frequency F x`. A singleton hypothesis
`{x} ∈ F` proves abundance of `x`. A pair hypothesis `{x,y} ∈ F` proves abundance
of `x` or `y`, including the degenerate `x = y` case.

**Proof and use.** Union with a fixed set is injective on inputs avoiding every
one of its elements. For a singleton, inject the avoiding subfamily into the
containing subfamily and partition `F`. For a pair, write `a,b,c,d` for the
cardinalities of the both, only-x, only-y and neither classes. Union with `{x,y}`
gives `d ≤ a`. The two frequencies are `a+b` and `a+c`, whose sum is at least
`a+b+c+d = F.card`; hence at least one is at least half. This eliminates singleton
and doubleton members from every counterexample and supplies a reusable counting
consumer. It does not find an abundant element in an arbitrary family.

**Declarations and source identity.** All names below have prefix
`GRWTSK.CombinatoricsAndDiscreteMathematics.SetFamilies.UnionClosedSmallMembers.`:
`UnionClosed`, `frequency`, `Abundant`, `Conjecture`,
`union_injective_on_avoiding`, `abundant_of_singleton_mem`,
`abundant_of_pair_mem`, `no_small_member_of_no_abundant`.
Source: `GRWTSK/CombinatoricsAndDiscreteMathematics/SetFamilies/UnionClosedSmallMembers.lean`,
Git blob `249ee949d9523efddd63875fd6427d0ba4f5a194` at the commit above.

**Evidence roles and obstruction.** Imported mathematics: the pinned Mathlib
finite-set cardinality and partition APIs. New kernel-verified bridges: none yet.
Source proof candidates: the four stated theorem declarations. Conditional
interfaces: `Conjecture` is a proposition, not an axiom or supplied proof.
Finite evidence and analogies: neither is used as proof acceptance. Unresolved:
there need not be a singleton or doubleton in an arbitrary nontrivial family.
Union with `{x,y}` on sets merely avoiding `x` is not generally injective:
for `x ≠ y`, the distinct sets `∅` and `{y}` have the same image. The required
selector or stronger local-configuration argument has not been supplied.
After recording that obstruction, this increment moved to Collatz.

### Collatz: an infinite restricted family and the precise descent premise

**Source.** Terence Tao, *Almost all orbits of the Collatz map attain almost
bounded values*, arXiv:1909.03562v7, 16 July 2026,
https://arxiv.org/abs/1909.03562v7, abstract definition of the unaccelerated map.
The initial version is from 2019 and the journal article appeared in 2022;
this entry pins v7 for its statement identity. No density theorem, probabilistic
method, or quantitative estimate from that paper is imported.

**Exact object, strategy and use.** `step n` is `n/2` for even `n` and `3*n+1`
for odd `n`, extended to zero to make a total natural-number function. `orbit k n`
is exactly `k` steps, and `ReachesOne n` means existence of a finite orbit witness.
`Conjecture` quantifies over every positive starting integer. `PositiveDescent`
requires for every `n > 1` a finite iterate strictly between zero and `n`.
Orbit concatenation prefixes a convergent orbit by a finite segment. A double
halves in one step; induction therefore proves convergence of powers of two
and of `2^k*n` whenever `n` already converges. Strong induction then proves
`Conjecture ↔ PositiveDescent`. The latter is an exact reduction exposing the
missing premise, not an independently discharged convergence argument.

**Declarations and source identity.** All names below have prefix
`GRWTSK.NumberTheory.ArithmeticDynamics.CollatzDescent.`:
`step`, `orbit`, `ReachesOne`, `Conjecture`, `PositiveDescent`, `orbit_add`,
`reachesOne_of_orbit`, `reachesOne_one`, `step_double`, `reachesOne_double`,
`reachesOne_pow_two`, `reachesOne_pow_two_mul`, `conjecture_iff_positiveDescent`.
Source: `GRWTSK/NumberTheory/ArithmeticDynamics/CollatzDescent.lean`,
Git blob `38c4029bd94d8f60cceafc34db5915600bce8266` at the commit above.

**Evidence roles and obstruction.** Imported boundary: pinned Lean core and its
arithmetic tactic, without Mathlib. New kernel-verified bridges: none yet.
Source proof candidates: the eight theorem declarations. Conditional interface:
the descent equivalence retains its universal premise. Finite evidence: no finite
search is claimed. Analogy: none promoted. Unresolved: strict positive descent
for every input above one. An almost-everywhere statement, including one in
logarithmic density, does not imply this universal obligation. No solution of
Collatz is claimed; the next research increment must move to another problem
or produce a new independently supported descent argument.

## Erdős–Moser small exponents and matching-compiler follow-up

Local attribution: grwtsk contributors. [Verification issue #1434](https://github.com/grwtsk/math/issues/1434).

**Source identity.** Bernd C. Kellner, *On stronger conjectures that imply the Erdös-Moser conjecture*, arXiv:1003.1646v2, 5 September 2012, https://arxiv.org/abs/1003.1646v2; journal publication 2011, https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jnt.2011.01.004. Only the abstract's equation is a statement reference; no stronger conjecture, paper theorem or method is imported. These elementary local proofs formalize known restricted cases without a novelty claim.

**Exact hypotheses and use.** `sumPowers k n` sums `1^k` through `n^k`; `Solution k n` means that sum equals `(n+1)^k`. Thus local `n` is the paper's `m-1`. For every natural `n`, `solution_one_iff` states `Solution 1 n ↔ n = 2`, and `not_solution_two` excludes `Solution 2 n`. The full `Conjecture` excludes all `k ≥ 2`, `n > 0`; it is a proposition, not an axiom. These results eliminate the first two exponents before considering the unresolved higher ones.

**Proof strategy and stopping boundary.** The linear sum exceeds the next integer at three summands, and its recursion preserves strict excess; the smaller inputs are evaluated directly. For `t ≥ 3`, the inequality `3*t ≤ t*t` gives `(t+1)^2 ≤ 2*t^2`. At four summands the square sum is `30 > 25`. Adding the next square preserves excess over the following square; induction covers every larger number of summands. The quadratic induction gives no uniform exclusion for `k ≥ 3`. No full open problem is solved.

**Fully qualified declarations and mathematical source pins.** Prefix `GRWTSK.NumberTheory.DiophantineEquations.ErdosMoserSmallExponents.`: definitions `sumPowers`, `Solution`, `Conjecture`; theorem candidates `linear_sum_gt`, `solution_one_iff`, `next_square_le_twice_square`, `square_sum_gt`, `not_solution_two`. Source path `GRWTSK/NumberTheory/DiophantineEquations/ErdosMoserSmallExponents.lean`, commit `682b62ec7cf4928049cc2a24966921c8518db489`, Git blob `5fe982a9ad2ca8f66b5aa781d2ab56695ddc7977`.

**Evidence roles.** Imported mathematics: installed pinned Lean core and its arithmetic tactic. Kernel-verified bridges: none yet. Source proof candidates: the five theorems above. Conditional interface: the full conjecture retains both hypotheses and is not discharged. Finite evidence: explicit small base cases occur inside the proposed inductive proofs; no finite computation is promoted to the universal conclusion. Analogies: none promoted. Missing bridge: all higher exponents, together with source-bound checking of these candidates.

## Collatz checking failure, repairs and source relocation

Local attribution: grwtsk contributors. Continuation of the preceding union-closed,
Collatz and Erdős–Moser source entries; paper identities, exact hypotheses and
mathematical stopping boundaries remain as recorded there.
[Verification issue #1434](https://github.com/grwtsk/math/issues/1434).

The first admitted managed replay, external `research/unsolved-mathematics/core-run-v4`,
actually compiled the original Collatz source at math
`682b62ec7cf4928049cc2a24966921c8518db489` and **FAILED**. It found three diagnostics:
`orbit_add` cannot close its zero case by reflexivity because `0+b` needs an
explicit simplification; `if_pos` is deprecated in Lean 4.34.1; and the specified
`Nat.mul_assoc` simp argument is unused. The latter two are errors under the
reviewed warnings-as-errors policy. Receipt SHA-256:
`209eb310eddbd05605cfeb4f38472ad4c0af5ca6881a89098e83e72efeeff530`. The receipt records `passed=false` and
`host_config_restored=true`; no selected contract completed. Diagnostic axiom
output included compiler-generated `sorryAx` from failed elaboration. That failed
output is rejected, not admitted as a theorem or committed as a proof premise.

The three repairs are published on `main` in
`f6c8eadedaf7703b37dbd3b56acdf372efc88d7c`, current source
`GRWTSK/NumberTheory/CollatzDescent.lean`, Git blob
`2fd3bfd370039cba453fda0a6b8031f299f222c9`. They explicitly simplify `Nat.zero_add`,
use `ite_eq_left` and omit the unused simp argument. Statements and hypotheses are
unchanged. Concurrent repository relocation moved the unchanged other sources to
`GRWTSK/NumberTheory/ErdosMoserSmallExponents.lean` (blob
`5fe982a9ad2ca8f66b5aa781d2ab56695ddc7977`) and
`GRWTSK/Combinatorics/UnionClosedSmallMembers.lean` (blob
`249ee949d9523efddd63875fd6427d0ba4f5a194`). All fully qualified declaration
namespaces in the earlier entries remain unchanged. These path mappings do not
transfer acceptance from historical source to repaired bytes.

Fresh managed preparation succeeded for `core-profile-v5.json` (Collatz and
Erdős–Moser) and `union-closed-profile-v5.json`, against the repaired source commit.
Together they select all seventeen earlier theorem names and explicitly bind
all seventeen expected theorem types. The first repaired-source retry,
`core-run-v5`, exhausted a 60-second activation wait after another queued owner
obtained admission; no Lean child ran in that retry. Its failure streams remain
retained. The successor `core-run-v6` uses the maintained CLI's explicit bounded
activation wait of 21600 seconds and the same immutable core registry. It grants
no lock preemption, resource change or mathematical acceptance.

### Union-closed bounded replay failure and arithmetic isolation (2026-10-29)

Henning Bruhn and Oliver Schaudt, *The journey of the union-closed sets conjecture*, arXiv:1309.3297v2 (2013), <https://arxiv.org/abs/1309.3297>, Sections 4–5, remains the statement source for the known singleton/doubleton cases attributed there to Sarvate and Renaud. For finite union-closed families, a singleton or doubleton member gives an element occurring in at least half the members; the counterexample obstruction retains exactly those hypotheses. This does not prove the general conjecture.

The four declarations `union_injective_on_avoiding`, `abundant_of_singleton_mem`, `abundant_of_pair_mem`, and `no_small_member_of_no_abundant` under `GRWTSK.CombinatoricsAndDiscreteMathematics.SetFamilies.UnionClosedSmallMembers` remain source proof candidates. Managed `union-closed-run-v7` at math `74aeb3c9021b441b9ef728d83540cdcca50cd871`, source blob `249ee949d9523efddd63875fd6427d0ba4f5a194`, exited 1: target Lean exited -6 with interpreter memory exhaustion under the unchanged 1024 MiB limit. Host restoration passed. Receipt SHA256 `83d82f75028d9080ef5bbae6418d2f4a0a462cddf2308cb040d46ac973655322`; target axiom audits and all four expected-type consumers: **NOT_RUN**.

Source commit `83841105907905141e384149de407ffd64cb3c50`, blob `0df97758ff3fbf3afce0ec8361430a4850302556`, isolates the two final counting arguments into private natural-number lemmas, preserving the four public statements and hypotheses. This is an unverified elaboration-cost mitigation, not a diagnosed or verified cure. Fresh source-bound replay, expected types and transitive axiom audit: **NOT_RUN**; another owner holds admission. The separately verified thirteen Collatz/Erdős–Moser declarations retain their earlier exact receipt scope.

## Johnston–Yang: complete coprime small-cofactor accounting

**Imported source.** Daniel R. Johnston and Andrew Yang, *Some explicit estimates for the error term in the prime number theorem*, arXiv:2204.01980v2, 20 April 2022 (document date 21 April), https://arxiv.org/pdf/2204.01980v2; DOI https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jmaa.2023.127460. Corollary 1.3 equation (1.6), page 2, all real x>=2: |pi(x)-li(x)|<=9.59 x(log x)^0.515 exp(-0.8274 sqrt(log x)); unshifted principal-value li from0. Primary pages1–2 inspected. No historical influence inferred.

**Local sources.** grwtsk contributors. Extended documentation/mathematics/NumberTheory/Moebius/prime-cofactor-negative-family.md, blob ef4864fc3dccc37eddb7ceb56b3d61aa9ae6cca0; new documentation/mathematics/NumberTheory/Moebius/complete-small-cofactor-family.md, blob f25eaf6626855e64814001bca8e93ce4ba7c0d33. Inspection base 57a2cad10d185dbb52890b96f5c98e04242b712c; source commit is the containing main commit. Notes pin exact source conversation/message/rendered-final hashes. Original N=2^j, frozen Gram/horizon, strict gap, terminal endpoint and gcd parameter d<=2^ceil(j/8) retained. Small cofactor primes j^2<ell<=j^8 avoid d; both squarefree cofactors are completed and coprime, exactly one prime>j^8 per residual core, with both large primes avoiding d and each other. Explicit sufficient inequalities, eventually valid uniformly in d, give a positive proper-family bound of order a(d)^2 N/(d^2(log N)^2), and the uniform asymptotic a(d)^2 N/(d^2 log^2(N/d))*(c2 J1/2+o(1)). Both source tails and the independently derived shared-large-prime correction are priced. No numerical starting threshold is certified.

**Classification and declarations.** Imported written mathematics: the pinned scalar PNT estimate only. New local written source proof candidates: finite-degree sign reversal, complete double-cofactor product plus actual-source Rankin comparison, shifted-log and full prime-error estimates, positive-family bound/asymptotic. Existing coefficient GRWTSK.NumberTheory.Moebius.PickupPrimeBlockSource.rough and stage selection GRWTSK.NumberTheory.Moebius.PickupGrowingPrimeStages.stages. New fully qualified Lean declarations NONE; kernel-verified bridges NONE claimed. No conditional axiom/certificate interface, finite evidence or analogy promoted to acceptance.

**Verification and precise missing bridges.** Compilation NOT_RUN; fresh kernel replay NOT_RUN; transitive axiom audit NOT_RUN. No proof execution dispatched. The original negative subfamily remains a valid proper-family theorem but is contained in the positive completed family and is not independent net credit. Zero/multiple-large-prime residual cores, rough primes outside the cofactor band, other Gram cells and outside-band source remain unbounded. Native formalization and the complete every-positive-rate signed RH estimate remain unresolved.


## 3Blue1Brown: elastic collision algebra and mass-weighted energy (first increment)

- **Scope/source:** Grant Sanderson, *How colliding blocks act like a beam of light...to compute pi.*, 2019-02-03, https://www.3blue1brown.com/lessons/clacks-via-light/ ; text adaptation credited by the source to Josh Pullen. Consulted 2026-10-28. The lesson is mutable; no immutable upstream web blob is claimed. The larger request includes all videos, Shorts, guest videos, talks and appearances elsewhere. No complete video or exhaustive inventory is claimed.
- **Local source pin:** `GRWTSK/Mechanics/Classical/ElasticCollision.lean`, blob `84e61612c43ddd36438cbc76481cc890d1c58995`; base math commit `4b0ff1e76e1b2b9b295562ca8554d4748249eef2`; containing publication commit pins the complete tree.
- **Exact objects:** real masses m,M and velocities u,v; first updated velocity `((m-M)u+2Mv)/(m+M)` and second `((M-m)v+2mu)/(m+M)`. Algebraic identities assume only `m+M ≠ 0`. The square-root coordinate bridge additionally assumes both masses nonnegative. Physically positive masses satisfy these joint premises; the source does not model finite-size blocks, contact times or trajectories.
- **Fully qualified declarations:** prefix `GRWTSK.Mechanics.Classical.ElasticCollision.` with definitions `first`, `second`; theorem candidates `momentum`, `twice_energy`, `relative_velocity`, `first_involution`, `second_involution`, `equal_mass_first`, `equal_mass_second`, `wall_twice_energy`, `scaled_energy_eq`, `scaled_energy_conserved`.
- **Proof and mathematical use:** clear the nonzero mass-sum denominator, then use polynomial identities. The update preserves momentum and twice kinetic energy, reverses relative velocity and is an involution. Equal nonzero masses exchange velocities. The coordinate map `(u,v) ↦ (sqrt(m)u,sqrt(M)v)` identifies twice kinetic energy with squared radius and transports energy conservation to that radius. This supplies an explicit representation bridge for a future geometric collision count, rather than assuming that bridge as a field.
- **Boundary:** conservation alone also permits the unchanged state; a collision law must select the nontrivial reflection. Zero total mass is excluded from the collision identities. Negative masses are excluded from the real square-root bridge. Wall reflection preserves energy but does not conserve the momentum of the two-block subsystem.
- **Evidence roles:** imported mathematics is Mathlib real arithmetic, square roots and tactics under existing pins. All ten local theorems are source proof candidates; kernel-verified bridges: none claimed. Conditional interfaces: no assumed collision-count theorem. Finite evidence and analogies used as proof: none. The optics interpretation is not yet a proved equivalence of trajectories.
- **Missing bridges:** uniqueness/physical selection of the collision update; admissible contact dynamics and wall events; mass-weighted reflection law; unfolding with event-count preservation; exact angle and rounding conventions; collision count and decimal digits of pi. These are distinct mathematical obligations. Coverage of the three-video sequence, let alone every video, remains incomplete.
- **Verification:** compilation `NOT_RUN`; fresh kernel checking `NOT_RUN`; transitive axiom auditing `NOT_RUN`. Proposed source-bound command through supported Noe tooling: `lake env lean GRWTSK/Mechanics/Classical/ElasticCollision.lean`, followed by fresh replay and `#print axioms` for each named theorem. No execution dispatched. Static discovery inventory: `documentation/mathematics/_verification/targets/three-blue-one-brown-source-inventory.json`; its 177 official lesson URLs are pending review, not 177 formalized videos. Root imports and dependency pins are unchanged.


## 3Blue1Brown colliding blocks: physical events, reflection and strict angular rounding

- **Sources/bylines:** Grant Sanderson, *The most unexpected answer to a counting puzzle* (2019-01-13), https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=HEfHFsfGXjs ; *Why do colliding blocks compute pi?* (2019-01-20), https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jsYwFizhncE ; *How colliding blocks act like a beam of light...to compute pi.* (2019-02-03), https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=brU5yLm9DZM . Official lesson pages are https://www.3blue1brown.com/lessons/clacks/ , https://www.3blue1brown.com/lessons/clacks-solution/ and https://www.3blue1brown.com/lessons/clacks-via-light/ . The first and third credit Josh Pullen for text adaptation. The later *Why colliding blocks compute pi* (2025-03-13), https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6dTyOl1fmDo , https://www.3blue1brown.com/lessons/colliding-blocks-v2/ , is included as a separate source version and credits Josh Pullen for text adaptation. Its date and upload identity were read from the official structured metadata. Video IDs were read from the official lesson HTML on 2026-10-28, not inferred from titles.
- **Paper:** G. Galperin, *Playing Pool with π (the Number π from a Billiard Point of View)*, 2003, DOI https://doi.org/10.1070/RD2003v008n04ABEH000252 , public PDF https://www.maths.tcd.ie/~lebed/Galperin.%20Playing%20pool%20with%20pi.pdf . Exact methods are Lemma 2(c) and sections 8–10: the integral angle endpoint, unfolding/count reduction, reciprocal-arctangent approximation, and the subsequent arithmetic questions/conjecture. The front-page digit theorem is not imported without the later qualifications. No claim is made about the present external resolution of that conjecture.
- **Source pins:** `GRWTSK/Mechanics/Classical/ElasticCollisionDynamics.lean`, blob `5572c411b021650b556f36c63ccb19e52a12f4ca`; `GRWTSK/Mechanics/Classical/CollisionAngleCount.lean`, blob `6df098b099eb624a3fa9b6418e850628c8c03195`. The containing publication commit supplies the complete source tree. The imported local `ElasticCollision.lean` remains blob `84e61612c43ddd36438cbc76481cc890d1c58995`, first published at `abe554299af332650b82c482aeab7b1401a1555d`.
- **Exact physical objects/hypotheses:** order bodies as near-wall/light and far-wall/heavy; point positions satisfy `0 ≤ x ≤ y`. Free flight advances positions at unchanged real velocities. An isolated event requires nonnegative time, admissible start, strictly contact-free open flight, the appropriate endpoint contact, strict incoming velocity and positive distance from the simultaneous-impact vertex. The event constructors do not silently assert general existence or deterministic next impact. Momentum and relative-velocity reversal uniquely determine the collision map when `m+M ≠ 0`. Square-root reflection uses both masses nonnegative and nonzero total mass. No physical claim for negative masses follows from an algebraic identity.
- **Physical declarations:** prefix `GRWTSK.Mechanics.Classical.ElasticCollision.`; `collision_law_unique`, `square_mass_reflection_first`, `square_mass_reflection_second`, `scaled_reflection_first`, `scaled_reflection_second`, `State`, `State.advance`, `State.admissible`, `State.openFlight`, `State.energy`, `Event`, `Step`, `advance_energy`, `step_energy`, `step_admissible`, `blocks_leave_separating`, `wall_leaves_outgoing`, `after_blocks_next_wall`, `after_wall_next_blocks`, `Trace`, `trace_energy`, `no_step_of_outgoing`, `equal_mass_three_impact_run`.
- **Proof/use:** the linear collision equations give uniqueness. In squared-mass coordinates polynomial identities give twice the parallel projection minus the input; substituting the nonnegative square roots returns that reflection to the original masses. Event constructors enforce contact and direction, imply nonpenetrating endpoints, preserve twice energy, and force alternating event types whenever a next event exists. Trace induction transports energy. The equal-mass theorem explicitly constructs three contact-free flights and impacts from `(x,y,u,v)=(1,2,0,-1)` to `(1,1,0,1)` and proves no subsequent event is possible; it does not yet cover arbitrary initial separations/speeds or establish uniqueness of all traces.
- **Exact angular objects/declarations:** prefix `GRWTSK.Mechanics.Classical.CollisionAngleCount.`; `angularCount θ = ceil(pi/θ)-1` as an integer; `le_angularCount_iff`, `angularCount_bounds`, `angularCount_eq_iff`, `angularCount_nonnegative`, `angularCount_error`, `angularCount_pi_div_four`, `massAngle`, `massAngle_pos`, `massAngle_lt_pi_div_two`. The mass angle is `arctan(sqrt(m/M))`, positive for positive masses. For positive θ, an integer n is the angular count exactly when `n*θ < pi ≤ (n+1)*θ`. The approximation is `0 < pi-N*θ ≤ θ`; this is not decimal-floor agreement.
- **Boundary/use:** ceil minus one handles the grazing integral endpoint: θ=pi/4 gives three, whereas an unconditional floor formula gives four. The angular model is independent of physical `Trace`; the missing event-count correspondence is not hidden as a theorem premise. Conservation does not select collision over unchanged velocities. Event alternation does not prove existence, termination or a count. A small angular approximation error does not by itself establish exact decimal digits.
- **Evidence roles:** imported mathematics is Mathlib arithmetic, square roots, real arctangent and integer ceiling under existing dependencies; the 23 new theorems are source proof candidates. Kernel-verified bridges: none claimed. Conditional interfaces: no assumed physical/angular count equivalence and no assumed all-exponents digit theorem. Finite evidence: the normalized three-impact example is represented by explicit source proof terms, still unchecked. Analogies as proof: none; the projection-reflection identity is explicit algebra, while a full equivalence of trajectories remains unproved.
- **Verification:** both new modules: compilation `NOT_RUN`, fresh kernel checking `NOT_RUN`, transitive axiom audit `NOT_RUN`. Intended supported Noe checks are `lake env lean GRWTSK/Mechanics/Classical/ElasticCollisionDynamics.lean` and `lake env lean GRWTSK/Mechanics/Classical/CollisionAngleCount.lean`, with named axiom checks and independent exact-type consumers for the three-impact run and angular interval characterization. A managed fresh-closure run of the ten original declarations at `abe554299af332650b82c482aeab7b1401a1555d` has been dispatched, but no terminal acceptance is asserted here; operational attempts and receipts remain outside math. Pending source-bound verification is tracked in https://github.com/grwtsk/math/issues/1439 .
- **Next mathematical bridge:** prove a deterministic first-impact/existence theorem for admissible initial states, then connect the alternating physical trace to a straight unfolded ray and the strict angular count. General termination/count, the demonstrated unequal-mass counts, parameter scaling, reciprocal-arctangent error bounds, and justified decimal statements remain open locally. None of the four video sources is marked fully formalized.

Galperin, *Playing Pool with π (the Number π from a Billiard Point of View)*, 2003, DOI https://doi.org/10.1070/RD2003v008n04ABEH000252 , https://www.maths.tcd.ie/~lebed/Galperin.%20Playing%20pool%20with%20pi.pdf , Lemma 2(c) and section 8. Folder heading is official MSC2020 70F35, *Collision of rigid or pseudo-rigid bodies*, under *Mechanics of particles and systems / Dynamics of a system of particles, including celestial mechanics*. Classification attribution remains Mathematical Reviews and zbMATH; this is a subject assignment, not proof acceptance. Declaration namespaces remain `GRWTSK.Mechanics.Classical.ElasticCollision`. - **Deterministic trace declarations:** namespace prefix `GRWTSK.Mechanics.Classical.ElasticCollision.` with `step_deterministic`, `Terminal`, `terminal_of_outgoing`, `initial_block_step`, `terminal_trace_length_unique`. Same-kind contact equations give a unique flight time; for competing kinds a midpoint after the earlier putative contact violates the later strict open flight, and equal times violate positive distance from the vertex. Deterministic first steps plus terminality give equal lengths for any two complete traces. Initial positions satisfy `0<a<b` and speed `c>0`; the first block Step is explicitly constructed at `(b-a)/c`. This construction does not assume that a subsequent impact exists.
- **Unfolding objects:** prefix `GRWTSK.Mechanics.Classical.ElasticCollision.Unfolding.` with `side`, `boundary`, `crossingTime`, `evenX`, `evenY`, `oddX`, `oddY`, `gap`, `evenState`, `oddState`. The unfolded affine ray is `(A-W*t,H)`; a ray boundary is `H*cos(alpha)-(A-W*t)*sin(alpha)` and its crossing time is `(A-H*cos(alpha)/sin(alpha))/W`. Nonzero/positive sine and speed hypotheses are retained. Crossing-time differences are `H*sin(beta-alpha)/(W*sin(alpha)*sin(beta))`; positive height and `0<alpha<beta<pi` give strict ordering.
- **Unfolding theorem candidates:** `boundary_at_crossing`, `boundary_affine`, `boundary_pos_iff`, `boundary_neg_iff`, `crossingTime_sub`, `crossingTime_strictMono`, `even_wall_side`, `even_block_side`, `odd_wall_side`, `odd_block_side`, `wall_switch`, `block_switch_first`, `block_switch_second`, `massAngle_cos`, `massAngle_sin`, `massAngle_cos_two`, `massAngle_sin_two`, `massAngle_gap`, `reflected_heavy_velocity`, `reflected_light_velocity`, `first_crossing_time`, `outgoing_of_weighted_sides`, `terminal_even_sides`, `terminal_odd_sides`, `counted_terminal_sides`, `evenState_advance`, `oddState_advance`, `interior_of_weighted_sides`, `even_segment_openFlight`, `odd_segment_openFlight`.
- **Mathematical use/hypotheses:** the fold maps retain signed wall and block gaps exactly. Sine/cosine of `atan(sqrt(m/M))` are derived for positive masses; geometric heavy-first reflection is then identified with the actual physical heavy/light collision outputs, with the coordinate swap explicit. The signed gap is a positive scalar times `y-x` (and similarly `v-u` for velocities). Both terminal parities give outgoing velocities for `N=ceil(pi/theta)-1`, including the integral grazing boundary. Folded affine states obey `State.advance`, and the open-flight theorems prove genuine strict noncontact throughout each interval between consecutive crossings. They do not take physical/angular event-count equivalence as a premise.

## Jaffe–Witten section 6.5: an actual finite periodic SU(2) Wilson probability

- **Public source and version:** Arthur Jaffe and Edward Witten, *Quantum Yang–Mills Theory*, published in *The Millennium Prize Problems*, American Mathematical Society, 2006; the publication year is confirmed by Arthur Jaffe's author-maintained bibliography https://www.arthurjaffe.com/Assets/documents/PapersToDownload.htm . The exact consulted artifact is the Clay-hosted fourteen-page PDF https://www.claymath.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/06/yangmills.pdf , section 6.5, PDF pages 11–12, consulted 2026-10-28. That artifact has no explicit revision stamp in the inspected text; no byte identity with another edition is asserted. The cited method is the compact-group, compactified finite lattice integral and its distinction from lattice-spacing/infinite-volume limits. No historical influence or researcher participation is inferred.
- **Local construction and normalization:** grwtsk contributors. Use the actual `Matrix.specialUnitaryGroup (Fin 2) ℂ`, not a finite surrogate gauge group. Vertices are `Fin 4 → ZMod L`, positive links are `Vertex L × Fin 4`, and plaquettes are a vertex and a pair `mu<nu`. The oriented square uses `U(x,mu) U(x+e_mu,nu) U(x+e_nu,mu)^(-1) U(x,nu)^(-1)`. Vertex gauges act by `h(x) U(x,mu) h(x+e_mu)^(-1)`. The penalty convention is explicitly `phi(g)=1-Re Tr(g)/2`; this local SU(2) normalization is not claimed to be a formula stated verbatim in the survey.
- **Exact hypotheses and boundaries:** the lattice group algebra is valid for every natural L. Finite inventories and their measure construction require `NeZero L`; the terminal existence consumer explicitly assumes `3<=L` and derives that instance. Partition-function bounds and probability require real beta>=0. Fixed-gauge measure invariance is proved for every real beta. Configurations and vertex gauges are the full actual finite products; no gauge fixing or quotient measure is substituted. All links in the oriented plaquette, including both inverse links, are retained.
- **Proof and actual consumer:** torus translations commute, so group cancellation gives plaquette conjugation covariance at the initial vertex. Matrix trace cyclicity gives action invariance. Actual unitarity bounds each matrix entry by one, hence real trace in[-2,2] and phi in[0,2]. The closed determinant-one unitary matrix subset is contained in the compact product of closed complex unit disks, proving actual compactness and topological-group instances. Normalize maintained Haar on the actual compact univ; mass is one. Right invariance is derived by compact Haar uniqueness and total mass one, with no commutativity claim. `Measure.pi` constructs the link product Haar probability. Its coordinatewise two-sided invariance proves actual gauge preservation. The finite action satisfies0<=S<=2*card(P). Bounded measurable integration yields exp(-2*beta*card(P))<=Z<=1 and Z>0. The actual measure is `nu.withDensity (ofReal (exp(-beta*S)/Z))`; its total mass one and gauge invariance are proved. No invariant measure, positive partition function or probability property is supplied as an unproved research field.
- **Exact source binding:** complete inspected base main `b870bf85c47e9e9bfedf3fc1fd31a2be60719e76`, tree `59ae81e242a9e1d51fd2462a92ec34cfe66e2c9b`; the six candidate source blobs below specify the overlay exactly. These are uploaded source candidates pending human integration, not a new main commit. Earlier bytes and external acceptance do not certify these candidates.

## Jaffe–Witten finite Wilson regulator: bond reflection and the Hermitian observable pairing

- **Public paper context:** Arthur Jaffe and Edward Witten, *Quantum Yang–Mills Theory*, published in *The Millennium Prize Problems*, American Mathematical Society, 2006; same Clay-hosted fourteen-page version, section 6.5, pages 11–12, https://www.claymath.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/06/yangmills.pdf . The earlier ledger entry records the author-maintained bibliography and version qualification. Section 6.5 separates finite lattice measures, reflection positivity and reconstruction, limits and a physical gap. This increment derives the user-specified finite bond-reflection identities locally; it does not import the survey's reflection-positivity statement without proof or infer historical influence or researcher participation.
- **Exact finite model:** use the already published actual matrix SU(2) Wilson density at period L=2*T with T>=2, real beta>=0, and time coordinate 0. Vertex reflection has time 1-x_0 modulo 2*T and fixed spatial coordinates. A forward temporal link is reflected to the inverse positive link based at theta(x)-e_0; a spatial link is pulled back to theta(x) without inversion. The complete link and plaquette inventories and the actual normalized product Haar measure are retained.
- **Reflection and action proof:** actual vertex and link involutions give the configuration involution and homeomorphism. At a temporal plaquette, put y=theta(x)-e_0 and A=U(y,0). The literal transformed holonomy is A^(-1)*H_U(y;0,i)^(-1)*A. Spatial plaquettes pull back at theta(x). Matrix inversion is star, so actual real-trace identities prove phi(g^(-1))=phi(g). Conjugation invariance and an explicit bijection of the complete plaquette inventory prove S(Theta U)=S(U), including orientation reversal and both periodic boundaries.
- **Actual measure proof:** inversion of the noncommutative SU(2) group is not used as a homomorphism. The inverse pushforward of the constructed Haar probability is left invariant by actual right invariance; compact Haar uniqueness and mass one prove inversion preservation. Selective temporal-coordinate inversion and finite coordinate permutation each preserve the actual product Haar measure. Their composition is literally Theta. The action equality then proves reflection invariance of the constructed Wilson density. Gauge invariance is not substituted for this proof. These symmetry identities work at every nonzero period and every real beta; probability/integrability usage retains beta>=0.
- **Positive-time algebra:** positive vertices have actual ZMod representatives 1,...,T. A positive interior link has both endpoints in that set. The concrete complex subalgebra of continuous functions consists of observables invariant under all actual vertex gauges and equal on configurations agreeing on those links. Constants, addition, multiplication and conjugation closure are proved. Temporal crossing layers based at 0 and T are explicitly excluded from observable dependence. At time T the target has representative T+1<2*T, proved using T>=2. The crossing variables remain in every full Haar/Wilson integration; no measure is projected or truncated. Reflection exchanges the positive and negative regions, so positive-algebra reflection stability is not assumed.
- **Actual consumer and hypotheses:** for T>=2, beta>=0 and members F,G of this concrete algebra, define B(F,G)=integral conjugate(F(Theta U))*G(U) dmu_beta,2T. Continuity on actual compact configuration space and the proved Wilson probability give integrability. Change of variables under the actual measure-preserving involution, followed by integral conjugation, gives B(F,G)=conjugate(B(G,F)) and Im B(F,F)=0; an equivalent equality identifies the diagonal with its real part. The positiveWilsonMeasure definition is exactly the existing period 2*T Wilson measure with NeZero derived from T>=2. No integrability or measure-invariance field is assumed. The source proves these pairing identities, not a packaged sesquilinear-form structure or a Hilbert norm.

## Jaffe–Witten finite Wilson regulator: local positive-type kernel and the two-boundary gluing frontier

- **Public paper context:** Arthur Jaffe and Edward Witten, *Quantum Yang–Mills Theory*, in *The Millennium Prize Problems*, American Mathematical Society, 2006; the same Clay-hosted fourteen-page source, section 6.5, pages 11–12, https://www.claymath.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/06/yangmills.pdf . The finite lattice/reflection-positivity context is retained. The elementary Frobenius/entrywise-series proof below is derived locally for the user's exact normalization; no theorem or historical influence is attributed to the survey without an explicit source argument, and no researcher participation is inferred.
- **Exact kernel and hypotheses:** the existing SU(2) is the determinant-one unitary matrix group on Fin 2 over Complex, with penalty(g)=1-Re Tr(g)/2. For every finite index type I (including empty), beta>=0, A:I->SU2 and arbitrary complex c:I->Complex, w_beta(A,B)=exp(-beta*penalty(A*B^(-1))). The actual ordered sum Q=sum_i sum_j conjugate(c_i)*c_j*w_beta(A_i,A_j) satisfies Im Q=0 and Re Q>=0. No injectivity, distinctness, coefficient reality or strict positivity is assumed.
- **Proof and normalization:** inversion is actual matrix star. All four complex entries, both real coordinates per entry, give Re Tr(A*B^*)=sum_k f(A)_k*f(B)_k in eight real coordinates. Thus w=exp(-beta)*exp((beta/2)*Gram). Multiplying an entrywise Gram power by one more Gram factor expands into a sum of the previous quadratic form with coordinate-weighted coefficients. Power zero is the all-ones kernel, not the identity matrix. Nonnegative Taylor coefficients and the scalar exponential-series limit at every entry, including negative Gram entries, give positivity of the entrywise exponential. For complex coefficients its real quadratic form is the sum of the real-coefficient forms of Re c and Im c; symmetry cancels the imaginary form. This is a scalar entrywise exponential, never a matrix exponential. Zero Taylor truncation is the zero kernel; the zeroth power is one. beta=0 is all ones. The explicit repeated-matrix family (A,A), coefficients (1,-1), has quadratic sum zero for every beta.
- **Literal plaquette consumer:** at x and spatial direction nu, define A=U(x,0)*U(x+e0,nu)*U(x+e_nu,0)^(-1) and B=U(x,nu). The existing oriented holonomy is exactly A*B^(-1), and its exact Boltzmann factor is w_beta(A,B). Both boundary temporal transports remain in A. The actual mu<nu plaquette inventory is used. BoundaryVertex T includes both actual time representatives 0 and T at L=2*T; the map is valid at either boundary. Kernel symmetry allows reversal of its two path arguments. Both paths transform by h_x on the left and h_(x+e_nu)^(-1) on the right; simultaneous bitransport invariance proves compatibility. Transport of only one argument is not discarded.

## Jaffe–Witten finite Wilson regulator: exact two-boundary conditional-Haar gluing

- **Public paper context:** Arthur Jaffe and Edward Witten, *Quantum Yang–Mills Theory*, in *The Millennium Prize Problems*, American Mathematical Society, 2006; same Clay-hosted fourteen-page source, section 6.5, pages 11–12, https://www.claymath.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/06/yangmills.pdf . - **Exact hypotheses and consumer:** natural T>=2, period L=2*T, real beta>=0, existing actual matrix SU(2), existing penalty 1-Re Tr(g)/2, normalized product Haar, the existing Wilson partition Z and measure, and F in the actual continuous complex gauge-invariant positive-time algebra. The terminal declaration `GRWTSK.Geometry.GaugeTheory.WilsonReflectionGluing.positiveReflectionForm_gluing` constructs NeZero(2*T) from T>=2; no invariant measure, gluing, factorization, observable decomposition or positivity certificate is assumed.
- **Literal link partition:** E+ is the existing two-endpoint positive interior predicate. E- is its actual reflected pullback. C is exactly the temporal links based at representatives 0 or T. Temporal E+ bases are 1,...,T-1, temporal E- bases T+1,...,2*T-1; spatial E+ bases are 1,...,T and spatial E- bases 0,T+1,...,2*T-1. Proved ZMod successor, negative and 1-minus representative formulas establish coverage and all pairwise exclusions, including wraparound and T=2. The actual finite bijection (E+ disjoint_union E+) disjoint_union C -> E sends the second positive copy through the maintained reflected-link involution.
- **Actual Haar disintegration:** J(u,v,t) retains u on E+, the reflected v on E- (inverse on temporal coordinates), and every actual t coordinate on C. Each finite sum split, index bijection and temporal inverse is proved measure preserving from the constructed one-link Haar probability. The full coordinate map is a measurable equivalence; its product-Haar change of variables and finite-probability/compact-continuity Fubini proofs give actual disintegration with the complete crossing integration. No coordinate is silently deleted.
- **Concrete conditional gauge:** h is inverse t on negative time0, t from the preceding time-T edge on negative timeT+1, identity on positive vertices and elsewhere. The previous-time map and all shifted endpoints are proved on the actual torus. At base0 the cancellation is t^(-1)*t; at baseT it is t*t^(-1). T>=2 makes the modular negative layers distinct. The induced second-half transformation has multipliers h(theta(source)) and h(theta(target))^(-1), acting on each coordinate by fixed left/right Haar translations for fixed t. An explicit measurable equivalence and actual product-Haar invariance supply its conditional change of variables.
- **Conditional integration theorem:** `GRWTSK.Geometry.GaugeTheory.WilsonBoundaryGaugeIntegration.two_boundary_gauge_haar_gluing` proves integral I(U) dnu(U) = integral integral I(J(u,v,1)) dnu+(u)dnu+(v) for continuous gauge-invariant I. Its proof first disintegrates every crossing t, then uses the fixed-t negative-half Haar translations, then uses the constructed crossing measure's mass one. It does not claim that the map collapsing t to1 preserves Haar. `reflection_gauge` proves Theta(gauge_h U)=gauge_(h composed with theta)(Theta U), including reversed temporal endpoint factors. This supplies actual gauge invariance of I_F(U)=exp(-beta*S(U))*conjugate(F(Theta U))*F(U), and `unnormalizedReflection_boundary_gluing` consumes the conditional theorem with that proved supplier.
- **Actual f and action partition:** f(u)=F(J(u,1,1)) is a definition, and the existing positive-link dependence proves F(J(u,v,t))=f(u). Full configuration equality proves Theta J(u,v,1)=J(v,u,1). A plaquette is wholly positive exactly when its first edge is positive because its second direction is spatial; all four vertex and link incidences are proved. The full action is the wholly-positive action plus its reflected action plus the sum over all actual temporal crossing plaquettes at both boundaries. The negative sum uses the existing reflected-plaquette bijection and literal penalty reflection theorem, including inverse orientation. Thus S(J(u,v,1))=S+(u)+S+(v)+S_cross(J(u,v,1)); no factorization field supplies this equality.
- **Boundary indices and orientation:** for a crossing plaquette b=(x;0,nu), ell_b is the positive spatial link (x+e0,nu) at time1 when x has representative0, and (x,nu) at timeT when x has representativeT. Both temporal boundary links are present before conditional gauge integration. At unit crossings the actual holonomy is u(ell_b)*v(ell_b)^(-1) at time0 and v(ell_b)*u(ell_b)^(-1) at timeT. The latter uses the already published exact kernel symmetry. Every member of the full mu<nu crossing-plaquette subtype occurs once in the product.

## Jaffe–Witten finite Wilson regulator: boundary-kernel integration and finite reflection positivity

- **Public paper context:** Arthur Jaffe and Edward Witten, *Quantum Yang–Mills Theory*, in *The Millennium Prize Problems*, American Mathematical Society, 2006; same Clay-hosted fourteen-page source, section 6.5, pages 11–12, https://www.claymath.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/06/yangmills.pdf . This stage derives the local finite-model reflection-positivity statement through actual feature moments and Haar integrals. No survey theorem, historical influence, researcher participation, continuum construction or mass-gap conclusion is inferred from the context.
- **Exact finite-model consumer:** natural T>=2, period2*T, real beta>=0, existing actual matrix SU(2), existing penalty 1-Re Tr(g)/2, the actual full Wilson measure/partition/reflection and F in the existing positiveTimeAlgebra T. The source theorem `GRWTSK.Geometry.GaugeTheory.WilsonReflectionPositivity.finiteWilson_reflection_positive` concludes Re(positiveReflectionForm T hT beta F F)>=0. NeZero(2*T) is constructed from T>=2 in its proof. No positivity, Schur-product, gluing, normalization, bounded-feature-margin or physical-gap certificate is assumed.
- **Exact concatenation and multiplicity:** BoundaryCoordinate T = CrossPlaquette T x RealCoordinate, retaining all eight real matrix-entry coordinates for every actual crossing plaquette at both boundaries. Phi(u)(b,k)=realCoordinates(u(boundaryPositiveLink b),k). Neither repeated positive spatial indices nor repeated feature words are deduplicated. With P=card(CrossPlaquette T), direct expansion of the existing kernel product gives K_boundary(u,v)=exp(-beta*P)*exp((beta/2)*Gram(Phi,u,v)). These are scalar entrywise exponentials. The actual SU(2) real-trace range supplies |Gram|<=2*P. The same identity consumes the existing generic finite exponential-Gram theorem to prove finite positive type for arbitrary finite complex coefficients, without a Schur-product bridge.
- **Integrated tensor words:** `FiniteRealGramIntegral.gram_pow_words` expands each n-th Gram power as the finite sum over all words w:Fin n->Coordinate of wordFeature(w,u)*wordFeature(w,v), where wordFeature is the actual product of coordinate values. Scalar complex-coefficient integration is exact: integral_(u,v) conjugate(g(v))*g(u)*Gram(Phi,u,v)^n dnu+(u)dnu+(v) = sum_w |integral_u g(u)*wordFeature(w,u) dnu+(u)|^2. Finite sum/integral interchange uses proved continuity and integrability on the actual compact probability space; actual product integration and integral conjugation produce each squared complex norm. The empty word contributes |integral g|^2, and the zeroth-power kernel is all ones, not the identity.
- **Taylor and limit premises paid:** beta/2>=0 makes every Taylor coefficient nonnegative, so the real part of every integrated truncation is nonnegative. The scalar-series convergence already written in the exact source is transported to Complex pointwise. The generic bound |Taylor_n(t*Gram)|<=exp(t*R), together with the continuous-map sup norm, gives the explicit integrable product-space constant ||g||_infinity^2*exp(t*R). In the actual model t=beta/2 and R=2*P, hence the valid fixed-T majorant is ||g||_infinity^2*exp(beta*P). Every truncation is AEStronglyMeasurable by actual continuity/integrability. The pinned Bochner dominated-convergence theorem and closed real order pass the integral inequality to the exponential. The source additionally proves |exp(-beta*P)*Taylor_n((beta/2)*Gram)|<=1. No positivity is inferred from pointwise K>=0, and no continuum-uniform coercivity or lower bound is supplied by these upper bounds.
- **Actual boundary integral theorem:** `GRWTSK.Geometry.GaugeTheory.WilsonBoundaryKernelIntegral.boundaryKernelProduct_integral_nonnegative` quantifies every continuous complex g on the actual HalfConfig T. It proves Re(integral_u integral_v conjugate(g(v))*g(u)*K_boundary(u,v) dnu+(v)dnu+(u))>=0. Its measure is exactly the existing normalized product Haar, with compact/Borel/second-countable structure inherited from the finite SU(2) coordinates. Full product and outer integrability are supplied separately; the double integral follows by actual Fubini. No new invariant-measure field is introduced.
- **Concrete weighted observable and return:** continuity of the finite positive plaquette action is proved from the actual four-link holonomies, fixed positive-plaquette inventory and SU(2) penalty. The existing J(u,1,1) map supplies continuity of halfAction and halfObservable. Thus g(u)=exp(-beta*halfAction(u))*halfObservable(F,u) is constructed as an actual continuous complex map, with single and product integrability proved. Its kernel integrand equals the published exact two-boundary gluing integrand, by real-weight conjugation and scalar exp_add. The existing gluing identity returns the nonnegative integral to Z*B(F,F). Actual Z>0 from the existing normalization theorem yields Re B(F,F)>=0. The companion properties theorem includes actual normalized integrability, zero imaginary diagonal and nonnegative real part.
- **Scope:** this is semidefiniteness for the specified finite periodic model, including beta=0, zero observables and possible null observables. No strictly positive norm, positive spectral margin, Hilbert quotient/completion, time evolution, vacuum/entire ground sector, infinite-volume/continuum scaling or physical mass gap is constructed. The first next form-construction step is to prove sesquilinearity of the actual B on the existing positive algebra, before Cauchy–Schwarz/null-radical and quotient/completion arguments.

## Finite SU(2) Wilson reflection form: sesquilinearity, radical and algebraic quotient

- **Paper context/bylines:** Arthur Jaffe and Edward Witten, *Quantum Yang--Mills Theory*, in *The Millennium Prize Problems*, AMS (2006), section 6.5, pp. 11--12; stable source https://www.claymath.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/06/yangmills.pdf. The paper distinguishes lattice regulators, reconstruction, infinite-volume/continuum limits and a physical mass gap. This increment prepares the finite reconstruction algebra from the exact maintained Wilson integral. The particular algebraic proofs below are local deductions, not an assertion that the paper formalizes them or that either author participated. Local attribution: grwtsk contributors. No historical influence is inferred.
- **Exact model and hypotheses:** fixed natural T>=2, period L=2*T, beta>=0, the existing actual SU(2) matrix group, Wilson penalty 1-Re Tr/2, normalized constructed Wilson measure, literal bond reflection, and the existing complex positiveTimeAlgebra T of continuous gauge-invariant observables depending on positive interior links. Every link, both crossing layers 0,T and the inherited Haar integrations remain present. No new measure, positivity or integrability field is assumed.
- **Actual integral laws and convention:** the mixed-product integrability theorem supplies each summand required by integral_add in either slot. Pointwise conjugation and the scalar integral identity give B(zF,G)=conj(z)B(F,G) and B(F,zG)=zB(F,G). The actual B is bundled as positiveTimeAlgebra T ->[starRingEnd Complex] (positiveTimeAlgebra T ->[Complex] Complex), namely a conjugate-linear first map with linear second maps. The scalar identities themselves hold for every real beta; the bundled additive map uses beta>=0 for actual mixed integrability.
- **Imported generic mathematics and its exact use:** Mathlib's semilinear-form convention retains Andreas Swerdlow's authorship; its pre-inner-product core and semidefinite Cauchy--Schwarz proof retain Zhouhang Zhou, Sébastien Gouëzel and Frédéric Dupuis's authorship. The submodule quotient and liftQ retain Johannes Hölzl, Mario Carneiro, Kevin Buzzard and Yury Kudryashov's authorship. All retain Apache-2.0 upstream license. The pinned PreInnerProductSpace.Core is used only as algebraic data for InnerProductSpace.Core.inner_mul_inner_self_le. Neither its toNorm nor any derived seminormed-group, topology or inner-product-space instance is installed on the already normed observable algebra.
- **Cauchy--Schwarz consumer:** the existing actual Hermiticity and finiteWilson_reflection_positive supply the core fields. Its pure algebraic inequality, together with norm-conjugation, gives norm(B(F,G))^2 <= Re B(F,F)*Re B(G,G). The maintained generic proof covers zero diagonals; no division by a diagonal is used, and its off-diagonal division occurs only after proving that scalar norm nonzero.
- **The actual radical:** nullSubmodule is the canonical LinearMap.ker of reflectionSesqForm. Cauchy--Schwarz proves membership iff Re B(F,F)=0. If the diagonal vanishes, norm(B(F,G))^2<=0 and hence B(F,G)=0 for every G; Hermiticity supplies the other slot. Thus the requested zero-diagonal set is proved to be a complex submodule, and both-slot null-addition invariance is an actual consumer. No positivity certificate or closure assumption defines it.
- **Actual algebraic quotient consumer:** ReflectionQuotient is the maintained Submodule quotient of the same positive-time algebra by that exact kernel. One liftQ descends each linear second slot using right annihilation; a second semilinear liftQ descends the first slot using left annihilation. quotientReflectionForm(mk F,mk G)=B(F,G) definitionally. The difference conditions F-F' in N and G-G' in N give representative independence. Quotient induction returns the inherited Hermiticity, real diagonal and nonnegativity to actual representatives. Re quotientReflectionForm(q,q)=0 iff q=0; the diagonal is strictly positive when q is nonzero. This is a vector-space quotient only: the null submodule is not asserted to be an ideal, and no quotient algebra is introduced.

## Finite SU(2) Wilson: two-step-margin translation descends to the null quotient

- **Paper context/bylines:** Arthur Jaffe and Edward Witten, *Quantum Yang--Mills Theory*, in *The Millennium Prize Problems*, AMS (2006), section 6.5, pp. 11--12; stable source https://www.claymath.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/06/yangmills.pdf. The source separates finite lattice regulators, reconstruction, infinite-volume/continuum limits and a physical gap. This increment is a local finite-model algebraic construction using the maintained Wilson measure and null quotient. It neither attributes these particular proofs to the paper nor infers historical influence or researcher participation. Local attribution: grwtsk contributors.
- **Actual hypotheses and source:** T is natural with T>=3, period L=2*T, beta>=0 for the radical/quotient consumers. The configuration, actual SU(2) matrix group, penalty 1-Re Tr/2, product Haar and normalized Wilson measure, bond reflection, positiveTimeAlgebra and nullSubmodule are unchanged. Translation and the unrestricted integral identity themselves hold at every nonzero period and every real beta as specified by their declarations. The exact existing mixed-observable integrability and finite reflection-positivity chain remain the suppliers for the positive form and radical.
- **Literal translation and incidences:** S U(x,mu)=U(x+e0,mu), with the inverse subtracting one modulo L. Explicit bijections of vertices, full positive links and full mu<nu plaquettes preserve directions and all inventories. Holonomy translation retains all four link factors, including both inverses. The vertex gauge becomes h'(x)=h(x+e0); commutation of the actual periodic shifts proves the target-endpoint equality. Temporal reflection retains its inverse link; the spatial and temporal base calculations separately prove S Theta S=Theta. Neither crossing layer 0,T nor any Haar variable is removed.
- **Actual measure construction consumed:** the link bijection instantiates the maintained proved product-Haar reindexing theorem. The plaquette bijection reindexes the complete literal action sum, giving action invariance. The existing Boltzmann-density theorem then proves preservation of the actual normalized Wilson measure. Gauge invariance is not substituted for this coordinate change, and no invariant-measure or translation certificate is assumed. The actual translation homeomorphism supplies its measurable embedding for the Bochner change of variables.
- **Two steps of support room:** twoStepDomain T is a complex submodule of the existing positive algebra, defined by dependence only on links whose TWO endpoints have natural time representatives in 1,...,T-2. The exact no-wrap calculation gives val(x+e0)=val(x)+1 and val(x+2e0)=val(x)+2 for those vertices. These values and the translated targets are positive and at most T. Consequently translatePositive and translateTwicePositive are separately constructed complex-linear maps from that same domain into the existing positive algebra. Continuity follows by composition; gauge invariance follows by the actual shifted vertex gauge. The second map does not iterate an operator assumed to preserve the margin domain.
- **Change-of-variables direction:** for arbitrary actual continuous F,G the substitution U=S V and proved S Theta S=Theta give B(tau F,G)=B(F,tau G). The restricted diagonal specialization uses G=tau F and gives B(tau F,tau F)=B(F,tau^2 F), where the required tau^2 F membership is supplied by the two-step endpoint proof. The one-step-margin region 1,...,T-1 does not by itself keep tau^2 F positive; that larger-domain implication is not asserted.
- **Genuine radical consumer:** if F in twoStepDomain has its original positive observable in N, the actual left-annihilation theorem applies to the actual positive member tau^2 F. The diagonal identity gives Re B(tau F,tau F)=0, and the proved null membership equivalence yields tau F in N. No whole-positive-algebra radical invariance is assumed.
- **Exact quotient domain and map:** classMap is the margin-submodule inclusion followed by the existing quotient map q. restrictedClasses is precisely its range in Q=A/N; membership is equivalent to existence of a margin representative F with q(F)=the given class. translatedClass is q composed with the proved first translation. Null preservation proves ker(classMap)<=ker(translatedClass), and in particular equal original classes from this domain have equal translated classes. The maintained liftQ and first isomorphism theorem then construct restrictedTranslation:restrictedClasses->Q. Its representative formula is restrictedTranslation(q(F))=q(tau F). This is a map from the specified image subspace into the full quotient, not an asserted endomorphism of that image or of Q; no representative outside the margin domain is used to define it.
- **Symmetric consumer on the actual common domain:** for q,r in restrictedClasses, quotientReflectionForm(restrictedTranslation(q),r)=quotientReflectionForm(q,restrictedTranslation(r)). The proof returns to margin representatives and the actual integral identity. Translated vectors are not claimed to stay in restrictedClasses. Finite volume does not make this continuous-observable domain finite dimensional, and no density is asserted.
- **Imported upstream mathematics:** the unchanged finite Wilson/reflection/positive-form chain and Mathlib periodic representative arithmetic, full finite sum reindexing, product measure permutation, Bochner change of variables, submodule quotient lift and first isomorphism theorem. The latter retain Johannes Hölzl, Mario Carneiro, Kevin Buzzard and Yury Kudryashov's authorship and Apache-2.0 license; imported product/Haar source retains Floris van Doorn and Sébastien Gouëzel's attribution. Other imported attributions and licenses remain intact.

A trace of length greater than N would split into that terminal prefix and a forbidden nonempty continuation. Therefore every actual prefix satisfies n<=N and n*theta<pi. The general optics trajectory/count theorem is not invoked to supply that bound, a matching trace or a certificate.

It assumes no mass positivity because this contact selection is kinematic; later consumers use positive masses. terminal_iff_outgoing therefore characterizes terminality by 0<=u<=v under those geometric hypotheses. Positive initial a,c give determinant -a*c, preserved nonzero through all actual prefixes.

## Sublinear exponent finite-prefix obstruction — source candidate (2026-10-29)

Local attribution: grwtsk contributors.

Primary context: Russell Impagliazzo and Ramamohan Paturi, *On the Complexity of
k-SAT*, Journal of Computer and System Sciences 62 (2001), 367–375,
[DOI 10.1006/jcss.2000.1727](https://doi.org/10.1006/jcss.2000.1727),
[author PDF](https://cseweb.ucsd.edu/~paturi/myPapers/pubs/ImpagliazzoPaturi_2001_jcss.pdf),
p.368, definition of the infimum exponential rate and Theorem 1. Also Russell
Impagliazzo and Ramamohan Paturi, *Exact Complexity and Satisfiability*, IPEC 2013,
LNCS 8246, pp.1–3,
[author PDF](https://cseweb.ucsd.edu/~paturi/myPapers/pubs/ImpagliazzoPaturi_2013_ipec.pdf),
p.2, distinction between complexity parameter and encoded instance size, and
exponential versus subexponential rates. These are contextual sources, not
imported or locally re-proved ETH theorems.

The new elementary local candidate is
`GRWTSK/ComputerScience/AlgorithmsInComputerScience/SublinearExponent.lean`.
Within namespace
`GRWTSK.ComputerScience.AlgorithmsInComputerScience.SublinearExponent`,
`IsSublinear f` means every positive integer rate `q` has an eventual threshold
beyond which `q*f(n) <= n`, with the exponent function fixed before those
quantifiers. `zero_isSublinear`, `delayedLinear_prefix`,
`delayedLinear_not_isSublinear`, and `finitePrefix_ambiguous` construct, for each
finite cutoff, two total natural-valued exponent sequences agreeing through that
cutoff while exactly one satisfies this eventual property. The counterexample
uses rate two and a point beyond both the observed cutoff and the proposed
threshold. The construction does not assume an algorithm or a SAT lower bound.
`noPrefixClassifier` then rules out any Boolean rule that is invariant under
that prefix observation and is correct for every exponent sequence; both
assumptions are explicit, with the concrete opposing witnesses supplied locally.

The same source now supplies `constant_isSublinear` and `IsSublinear.affine`.
For every fixed natural scale and offset, the latter transports the reciprocal-
rate bound through `n -> scale*n + offset`. Scale zero is handled as a constant;
for positive scale the proof samples source rate `q*(scale+offset)`, uses a
threshold at least one, bounds the enlarged parameter above by
`(scale+offset)*n`, and cancels the positive factor. This is local exponent
arithmetic, not a sparsification lemma or an algorithmic ETH reduction. The
polynomial encoded-input-size factor and total reduction time remain unmodeled.

`IsSublinear.add` requests both source bounds at reciprocal rate `2*q`,
combines their thresholds by addition, adds the two inequalities and cancels a
positive factor two. It requires no monotonicity or positive exponent values.
`IsSublinear.affine_add` consumes that result with the fixed affine transport
lemma. A separately established sublinear overhead bound remains an explicit
premise; no preprocessing estimate, machine model or SAT reduction is supplied.

Classification: **source proof candidates; native compilation, fresh kernel
replay and transitive axiom audit NOT_RUN**. Static target:
`documentation/mathematics/_verification/targets/sublinear-exponent-finite-prefix-v1.json`,
with empty allowed axioms and a 30-second compiler wall cap. The source and static target are bound to Math commit
`213b04a8aba70f7ecdef3a3f69a5bf4c73768e40`. Noe's cycle-134 evidence records the separate
publication and verification outcomes. Neither publication nor paper metadata
supplies acceptance. Source Git blob
`0692958f3dac01b118fca6b91fd46f67062acc81`, SHA256
`70ac54d713c9070930e6088a074481c8198abadea9bcc3612036ac9d7c00dc71`; profile Git blob
`bde14dcd895fe6c83c538757d371a948063a5755`. These byte identities do not establish compilation, kernel replay or axiom
acceptance.

Missing bridges: a formal machine/algorithm universe, correct uniform SAT
